# Pulsix v0.2c Automation Core Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Ship the v0.2c half of the automation design: schedules with guards and overrides, incoming webhook triggers for job templates and Git repositories, and the Workers page with token enrollment, certificate renewal and revocation. It ends with an E2E that enrolls a worker by token, fires a schedule with an injected clock, receives a signed webhook and revokes the worker.

**Architecture:** Schedules and webhook triggers are two new domain packages (`internal/schedules`, `internal/triggers`). Both enqueue through one new job-queue entry point, `jobs.Service.EnqueueRunWith`. It carries the trigger kind, the trigger reference and per-run overrides that `templates.Service` has validated. The overrides are sealed into the job's payload and merged over the template at claim time. A 15 s ticker picks due schedules with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, so several managers need no leader. Webhooks are two huma routes outside the org group. They resolve the trigger through a bypass-scoped lookup and run a fixed check pipeline, and a shared token bucket in Postgres enforces the rate limit. Worker enrollment adds an unauthenticated `Enroll` RPC (the TLS layer switches to `VerifyClientCertIfGiven`, and the interceptor still requires a certificate on every other method), a `worker_certificates` table that the interceptor consults on every RPC, 90-day leaf certificates with worker-driven renewal, and revocation that cuts open streams and requeues the worker's jobs.

**Tech Stack:** Go 1.27, chi v5, huma v2.39, pgx v5, sqlc 1.31, goose v3, gRPC + buf, testcontainers-go (TimescaleDB pg18), React 19, TanStack Router/Query, Tailwind v4, Vitest 5. New dependency: `github.com/theory/jsonpath` v0.12.1 (RFC 9535). The cron evaluator is our own (`internal/cronexpr`); see "Dependency decisions".

## Where this plan sits

| Plan | Backlog items | Delivers |
|------|---------------|----------|
| v0.2a Inventory (merged) | 2.1–2.7b | Groups, schema builder, credentials, jump hosts, preview |
| v0.2b Execution (merged) | 2.7c–2.12a, 2.14–2.53 | CA, gRPC/mTLS, queue, repos, templates, runs, bootstrap, rotation |
| **v0.2c Automation core** (this plan) | 2.13, 2.13b, 2.8 push webhook, 2.23, 2.24 | Schedules, webhook triggers (templates and repos), Workers page, token enrollment, renewal, revocation |
| v0.2d Notifications & calendar | 2.13a, 2.13c | Channels, maintenance windows and guard, schedule notifications, digest, activity filters, calendar |

Design: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-18-v0.2c-automation-design.md` §2, §3, §4 and the v0.2c parts of §9–§11. Everything in §5–§8 is v0.2d and out of scope. The `guards` jsonb stays open for v0.2d's `maintenance` key, which this plan does not implement.

## Global Constraints

- Language: every file in the repository is English. Chat with the user in German.
- Commits: Conventional Commits (`feat`, `fix`, `docs`, `refactor`, `perf`, `test`, `build`, `ci`, `chore`). End every message with the footer `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>`. Commit only what a task tells you to commit. Work happens on branch `feat/v0.2c-automation`, and history on `main` stays linear. Never push.
- Go 1.27, module `github.com/noixdev/pulsix`. Code must be `gofmt` and `golangci-lint run` clean. `go test -race -timeout 900s ./...` must be green before every commit. **Every `go test` command in this plan carries `-race -timeout 900s`.** The internal/api suite needs the timeout, and the flag is kept uniform everywhere.
- IDs are UUIDv7 (`uuid.Must(uuid.NewV7())`, or `uuid.NewV7FromReader(rand)` in services with injected entropy).
- **Layering (CLAUDE.md):** `internal/api` handlers only decode, call a service and encode. Business rules live in domain services that depend on interfaces declared next to them. Stores are sqlc-generated `internal/db/gen` behind a small interface. Handlers never touch `gen.Queries`. There is no package-level state beyond compiled regexps and constants. Time and randomness are injected. Aim for one responsibility per file; a file over ~400 lines is a signal to split. `internal/jobs/queue_sql.go` (1126 lines) and `internal/jobs/service.go` (891 lines) only gain call sites: new logic goes into new files.
- **Migrations are additive only.** The numbers 00024–00027 belong to this plan: `00024_job_trigger_ref`, `00025_schedules`, `00026_job_triggers`, `00027_worker_identity`. Every migration has a working `-- +goose Down`, because `migrations_roundtrip_test.go` rolls back to version 15 and re-applies. New tables need no GRANT, because the default privileges in 00002 cover them.
- Every new org-owned table has `org_id uuid NOT NULL`, `ENABLE` + `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` and the policy pair verbatim: `org_isolation USING (org_id = NULLIF(current_setting('pulsix.org_id', true), '')::uuid)` and `org_isolation_bypass USING (current_setting('pulsix.org_id', true) IS NULL OR current_setting('pulsix.org_id', true) = '')`. `rls_catalog_test.go` finds these tables on its own, and nothing is added to its exemption map.
- References between org-owned rows use composite foreign keys `(org_id, x_id) REFERENCES t(org_id, id)`.
- Stores are built on `*db.DB`. The three bypass-scoped paths in this plan run on an unbound context or the raw pool, and each of their statements filters by `org_id` explicitly (except the lookup by the secret-like key itself): the schedule ticker's due selection, the webhook trigger lookup by id, and the enrollment lookup by id. The existing job reaper follows the same pattern.
- Secrets at rest go through `crypto.Box.SealAAD`. The AADs are `schedule:<org>:<id>` (schedule extra-var overrides), `trigger:<org>:<id>` (webhook secrets) and `job:<org>:<id>` (the existing `jobs.JobAAD`, reused for run overrides). Secret values never appear in an API response (except a webhook secret, shown once at creation and rotation), a log line, an audit summary or meta, or a delivery row. The request body of a webhook is never stored.
- **Tokens, secrets and signatures are compared in constant time** (`hmac.Equal`, `crypto/subtle.ConstantTimeCompare`), including the enrollment secret hash.
- Every mutating org-scoped HTTP route carries `withAudit(operationID, objectType)`. The two hook routes and the schedule preview are allowlisted in `internal/api/audit_coverage_test.go` with a reason. The hook routes are audited by the triggers service itself (actor `webhook:<trigger-id>` + source IP), and the preview mutates nothing.
- Permissions (`internal/tenancy/roles.go`): schedules use `templates.read` / `templates.write`, and "run now" also needs `jobs.run`. Template triggers use `templates.read` / `templates.write`, repo triggers use `repos.read` / `repos.write`. New: `workers.read` and `workers.write`, both with minimum role `admin`, so owner and admin have them.
- Routes: org-scoped routes are `/api/v1/orgs/{org}/...`. The hook endpoints are `POST /api/v1/hooks/jobs/{id}` and `POST /api/v1/hooks/repos/{id}`, registered on the root API. They sit outside the org group, the bearer middleware and any session requirement. The API has no CSRF middleware, so none needs exempting.
- Coverage gates (`scripts/coverage-gate.sh`): every Go package ≥ 80 %. `internal/auth`, `internal/tenancy`, `internal/db`, `internal/ca`, `internal/credentials`, `internal/jobs`, `internal/worker/runner`, `internal/worker/sshprobe` and the inventory packages ≥ 90 %. `internal/jobs` currently sits at 90.4 % and stays ≥ 90 %. This plan adds `internal/cronexpr`, `internal/clientip` and `internal/enrolltoken` to the 90 % list, because they are pure security- and correctness-critical code. SPA ≥ 70 % (Vitest thresholds unchanged).
- Status colours (`ok #34D399`, `warn #FBBF24`, `crit #F87171`, `unknown #6B7280`) are reserved for states, and the accent `#00E5FF` is for actions and selection. Cron expressions, URLs, tokens, hostnames and variable names use JetBrains Mono (`font-mono`).
- **Dependencies are current.** Use the newest stable release, verified at implementation time (`go list -m -versions`, `npm view`), and record the pin in the task report. The TypeScript 5.9 pin in `docs/dependency-exceptions.md` still applies.
- **Docs:** end-user documentation lives in `~/_dev/noix-docs` on branch `pulsix`, with local commits only, **never pushed**. Public texts never name the tech stack: no gRPC, Postgres, Go, sqlc, huma, React, "mTLS library" and so on. Words like "certificate", "token", "cron expression" and "JSONPath" are product concepts and allowed. Each user-visible task edits its docs page and commits it in the docs repo. The final task reviews them all and updates the changelog.
- The generated SPA client (`web/src/api/schema.d.ts`) is regenerated with `cd web && npm run api:gen` in every task that changes the API, and committed with it. CI fails on drift.

## Decisions taken in this plan

- **Cron evaluation is our own `internal/cronexpr`**, a 5-field parser plus `@hourly @daily @weekly @monthly`. The alternative libraries were checked on 2026-09-18 against the spec's DST rules; see "Dependency decisions". None of them implements the rules in its `Next` function. The evaluator iterates wall-clock minutes in the schedule's zone. A wall time that falls in a spring-forward gap resolves to the first valid minute after the gap (`time.Time.ZoneBounds` start). A repeated fall-back wall time resolves to its first instant. Day-of-month and day-of-week combine with **OR** when both are restricted, as in Vixie cron (the crontab semantics operators expect), and a field starting with `*` counts as unrestricted.
- **Simple mode** has five kinds that compile to cron on save: `every_minutes` (1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 10, 12, 15, 20 or 30 — the divisors of 60, because cron cannot express other intervals exactly), `every_hours` (1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8 or 12), `daily {time}`, `weekly {days, time}` and `monthly {day 1–31, time}`. Months without that day are skipped, as in cron. The JSON form is `{"kind":"weekly","days":["tue","thu"],"time":"03:00"}`, stored verbatim in `schedules.simple`.
- **One human-readable text** is produced server-side: `cronexpr.Describe(spec)` returns text for expressions in a simple shape and `""` otherwise, and the UI shows the raw expression then.
- **The schedule domain is a new package, `internal/schedules`** (service, store, ticker). The existing `internal/schedule` package keeps only the connection re-test. Merging the two would mix two unrelated tickers.
- **Jobs keep their existing `trigger` column as the trigger kind.** It gains a CHECK (`manual`, `schedule`, `webhook`, `system`) and a new nullable `trigger_ref uuid` holds the schedule or trigger id. The spec says jobs "gain `trigger_kind`", but a second column with the same meaning as `trigger` would be redundant, so the existing column is reused (additive migration). The API exposes `trigger` (enum widened) and `trigger_ref`.
- **Per-run overrides travel sealed on the job.** `jobs.RunOverrides{Limit *string, ExtraVars map[string]any}` is JSON-encoded, sealed with `jobs.JobAAD`, and stored in `jobs.payload` for each per-zone job of the run. The claim opens it, replaces the template's `limit` and overlays the top-level extra vars, then applies the 2.36 denylist again and adds redaction for secret-typed values. The payload is wiped when the job ends, as today, so **retry-failed of a run that had overrides re-runs with the template's own limit and extra vars.** Keeping override secrets past the run would contradict the payload wipe, and the run view says so next to the retry button.
- **Override validation lives in `templates`.** `ValidateOverrides` checks the limit rules, the denylist, secret sealing (schedule save) or refusal (webhook mapping) and strict schema validation of the *merged* vars (template values overlaid with the override), so required fields satisfied by the template still pass. `RevealOverrides` opens a schedule's sealed values at fire time and validates again against the template as it is then. A schema change that made a stored override invalid records the scheduled run as `failed` with the reason "the schedule's overrides no longer match the template".
- **Ticker atomicity is at-least-once.** The run is enqueued through the normal org-scoped services (their own connections), while the `schedule_runs` row and the advance of `next_run_at` are written in the ticker's transaction. If that commit fails after the enqueue, the next tick fires again. That is the same at-least-once guarantee the reaper gives, and it is documented. The ticker never touches `schedules.revision` or `updated_at`, so an operator's If-Match stays valid across ticks.
- **Late runs and catch-up.** A due schedule is *late* when `now - next_run_at > 2 × tick`. The evaluator then counts the occurrences in `[next_run_at, now]` and takes the most recent one. If that one is itself within the grace period, the run is triggered normally, with reason "N earlier occurrence(s) missed" when N > 0. Otherwise it is triggered only with `catch_up.enabled` and `now - latest ≤ window` (reason "catch-up of N missed occurrence(s)"), and recorded as one `missed` row in every other case. `next_run_at` is always recomputed from `now`.
- **`schedule_runs.outcome` has a named CHECK** (`schedule_runs_outcome_check`). v0.2d widens it for `skipped_maintenance` by dropping and re-adding it. That relaxes the constraint and deletes no data. `trigger_deliveries.outcome` works the same way and gains **`failed`** beyond the spec's list, used when the enqueue itself fails (for example an empty scope). The HTTP answer is then `409` with a generic reason.
- **Webhook presets.** `pulsix` (HMAC, `X-Pulsix-Signature-256: sha256=<hex>`), `github` (HMAC, `X-Hub-Signature-256: sha256=<hex>`), `gitea` (HMAC, `X-Gitea-Signature: <hex>`), `gitlab` (token, `X-Gitlab-Token: <token>`), `bearer` (token, `Authorization: Bearer <token>`). A `preset` column stores the choice, and `auth`/`signature_header` are derived from it. **When a timestamp header is configured, the HMAC covers `<timestamp>.<raw body>`**, so the replay window cannot be dodged by replaying the body with a fresh timestamp. Without one, it covers the raw body. Only JSON bodies are mapped. A GitHub hook must use content type `application/json`, and the docs say so.
- **"Test call" runs in-process**: `triggers.Service.TestCall` signs a body (default `{}`) with the real secret and passes it through the same `Receive` pipeline, with source IP `127.0.0.1` and the IP allowlist skipped. That is the one difference, and the delivery row says "test call". A loopback HTTP request to `PULSIX_PUBLIC_URL` would fail behind many reverse proxies (hairpin NAT). A test call enqueues a real run, and the button says so.
- **Rate limit** `{"count": 10, "per": "1m"}`: count 1–1000, per 1 s–24 h. It is implemented as a token bucket in `trigger_buckets` (capacity = count, refill = count/per), with one conditional `UPDATE … RETURNING` that several managers share. A miss reads the bucket once to compute `Retry-After` (whole seconds, at least 1). Changing the rate limit refills the bucket.
- **Rejected deliveries are throttled** to one row per trigger, outcome and minute through an `INSERT … WHERE NOT EXISTS`. That covers `rejected_ip`, `rejected_auth`, `disabled`, `rate_limited` and `invalid_payload`. Deliveries for unknown ids record nothing (there is no trigger to attach them to). Each insert trims the trigger's rows beyond the newest 500.
- **Per-address limits** (spec §9) use one in-process fixed-window counter, `clientip.Limiter`. The hook endpoints allow 300 deliveries per minute and source address, summed over every trigger. That is generous, because GitHub and other CI systems deliver from shared address pools. The check runs before the trigger lookup, answers 429 with `Retry-After: 60` and records nothing, so a flood of unknown ids costs no database write. `Enroll` allows 10 calls per minute and address. Each manager counts on its own; the per-trigger token bucket is the limit that holds across managers.
- **Client IP.** `internal/clientip.Resolver` implements `PULSIX_TRUSTED_PROXIES`. When the TCP peer is inside the list, it walks `X-Forwarded-For` from the right and returns the first address that is not a trusted proxy. An unparsable entry stops the walk and returns the nearest address seen so far, which never trusts a spoofed value. The HTTP audit middleware now records `meta.source_ip` for every audited route, webhook deliveries record it, and the gRPC `Enroll` rate limiter keys on it (peer address plus `x-forwarded-for` metadata).
- **The CA is always built**, even with `PULSIX_GRPC_LISTEN` empty, because enrollment tokens embed its fingerprint. Creating an enrollment when the worker endpoint is disabled answers 409 "the worker endpoint is disabled (PULSIX_GRPC_LISTEN)".
- **Certificates.** All leaves are now valid for 90 days (was 825), and existing certificates keep theirs. The gRPC server certificate is re-issued in memory once less than a third of its validity remains (`tls.Config.GetCertificate`), because a manager running for more than 90 days would otherwise serve an expired certificate. The server sends `[leaf, CA]`, so a token-enrolling worker can pin the CA. CSRs may carry Ed25519, ECDSA P-256 or **ECDSA P-384** keys. P-384 is added beyond the spec's list because every bundle issued so far by `pulsix-manager worker add` carries a P-384 key, and those workers must be able to renew. CSR extensions and subject are ignored. The CA sets the CN (`pulsix-worker <name>`) and the org and zone URI SANs.
- **Renewal reuses the worker's key.** The CSR is signed by the current key and only `cert.pem` is replaced (temp file + rename in the same directory), so the key/certificate pair on disk is never torn. Keys rotate on re-enrollment. The compose `pulsix-worker-bundle` volume becomes writable for the worker (`:ro` dropped), so a renewed certificate survives restarts, and `worker-enroll` keeps reusing the renewed bundle because its serial is registered in `worker_certificates`.
- **The worker's identity directory** is `PULSIX_WORKER_IDENTITY_DIR`, default `<PULSIX_WORKER_DATA_DIR>/identity`. `PULSIX_WORKER_CERT`/`KEY`/`CA_CERT` stay supported as an all-or-none set (the CLI bundle). On start, a usable bundle wins and `PULSIX_ENROLL_TOKEN` is ignored with an info log. Otherwise the worker enrolls with the token and writes `key.pem` (0600), `ca.pem` and `cert.pem` (last).
- **Revocation.** It sets `workers.status = 'revoked'`, `workers.revoked_at` and `revoked_at` on every certificate of the worker in one transaction. Afterwards, the in-process `grpcsrv.StreamRegistry` ends the worker's open `RunEvents` streams with `Unauthenticated`. Its claimed and running jobs are requeued when `attempts < PULSIX_JOB_MAX_ATTEMPTS` (the claim already counted the attempt), with `error = 'requeued: worker revoked'` until the next finish overwrites it. The exhausted ones end as `error` "worker revoked" and cancelling ones as `cancelled`. The interceptor maps unknown **and** revoked serials to `Unauthenticated` (was `PermissionDenied`) and also refuses a certificate whose zone SAN differs from the registry row. A revoked worker's already running ansible process cannot be stopped from the manager. Its results are refused, and the requeued job runs elsewhere. With several managers (v0.6), a revocation cuts streams only on the manager that served it, while every manager refuses the worker's next RPC.
- **`workers.cert_serial` stays** (NOT NULL, UNIQUE), is set to the first certificate's serial and is no longer read. Lookups go through `worker_certificates`, and the backfill copies every existing serial with `not_before = created_at`, `not_after = created_at + 825 days`. That approximation is display-only; the worker renews based on its real certificate. `Touch` now updates by worker id, because a renewed certificate has a new serial.
- **The embedded worker** used to register a new `embedded` row on every manager start, which failed on the second start with `ErrNameTaken` (the table is `UNIQUE (org_id, name)`). It now reuses its row and attaches the fresh certificate with `workers.Service.AddCertificate`. A revoked embedded worker stays down, with an error log.
- **SPA placement.** The SPA has no "Repositories / Job templates / Editor" tab strip; those are sidebar entries. **Schedules** becomes a sidebar entry after Templates (`/o/$org/schedules`, the mockup's list plus detail panel), and each template row links to its schedules (`?template=<id>`). There is no template or repo detail page, so the **Triggers card** is a wide dialog opened from a "Triggers" row action on the templates page and on the repositories page. **Workers** is a top-level sidebar entry as in the mockup shell. The mockup's week calendar and notification rules are v0.2d.
- **Run view.** `trigger` shows as "Manual", "Schedule", "Webhook" or "System". For schedule runs the "Triggered by" fact links to `/o/$org/schedules?id=<trigger_ref>`. There is no name join in the job query (YAGNI; the link resolves it).
- **Audit actors.** `audit.ActorKind` gains `schedule` and `webhook`, and the `actor_kind` column is free text, so no migration is needed. Scheduled runs record `ActorKind=schedule, ActorID=<schedule id>, Action="schedule.run", ObjectType="job_template", ObjectID=<template id>, Meta={schedule_id, template_id, created_by, outcome, job_group_id}`. Webhook runs record `ActorKind=webhook, ActorID=<trigger id>, Action="webhook.run"`, with object `job_template` or `repo`, and `Meta={trigger_id, source_ip, job_group_id}`. Pause, resume, run now, pause all, rotate secret and test call are audited HTTP routes.

## Dependency decisions

Checked on 2026-09-18 with `go list -m -versions`, `gh api repos/<r>` (last push, last release) and a DST probe in Europe/Berlin (spring-forward 2026-03-29 02:00→03:00, fall-back 2026-10-25 03:00→02:00):

| Candidate | Latest | Maintenance | DST result | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `github.com/netresearch/go-cron` | v0.16.0 (2026-08-26) | active (commits 2026-09-10) | `30 2 * * *` fires **twice** on fall-back day (02:30 CEST and 02:30 CET); spring-forward normalizes to 03:30, not 03:00; DOM/DOW default AND | rejected: violates spec §2 DST rules |
| `github.com/adhocore/gronx` | v1.20.4 (2026-09-17) | active | skips the spring-forward occurrence entirely; picks the *second* fall-back occurrence | rejected |
| `github.com/hashicorp/cronexpr` | v1.1.3 (2025-09-04) | low activity | skips spring-forward; fires twice on fall-back | rejected |
| `github.com/robfig/cron` | — | unmaintained since 2020 | — | excluded by the spec |

→ **Own evaluator `internal/cronexpr`** (~250 lines, table-driven tests, 90 % gate). It is also the evaluator v0.2d's recurring maintenance windows and calendar use.

| JSONPath candidate | Latest | Notes | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| `github.com/theory/jsonpath` | **v0.12.1** (2026-08-30) | RFC 9535 implementation, MIT, active; exposes the parsed query (`Path.Query().Segments()`, `Segment.IsDescendant()`, `spec.Name`/`spec.Index`/`spec.WildcardSelector`) so the subset can be enforced after parsing | **chosen** |
| `github.com/ohler55/ojg` | v1.28.6 | JSONPath dialect predates RFC 9535 | rejected |
| `github.com/PaesslerAG/jsonpath` | v0.1.1 | stale, not RFC 9535 | rejected |

The subset (spec §3) is enforced on the parsed query: root `$`, then child segments only, each with exactly one selector of type name, index or wildcard. Descendant segments, filters, slices and multi-selector segments are refused at save time.

## File structure (v0.2c)

```
internal/clientip/{clientip,limiter}.go           trusted-proxy client IP resolution, per-address limiter
internal/cronexpr/{parse,next,simple}.go          cron parser, DST-correct Next, simple mode + Describe
internal/templates/overrides.go                   ValidateOverrides / RevealOverrides / SecretFields
internal/jobs/{run_request,run_overrides,revoke_requeue}.go
                                                  EnqueueRunWith/EnqueueSyncWith/HasActive*, claim-time merge, revocation requeue
internal/schedules/{schedule,service,validate,decide,ticker,store_sql,due_sql}.go
internal/triggers/{trigger,mapping,auth,service,receive,store_sql}.go
internal/enrolltoken/token.go                     pxw1.<id>.<secret>.<ca-sha256>
internal/ca/csr.go                                SignCSR, ParseLeaf, CAPEM, Pin (and 90-day leaves in ca.go)
internal/workers/{enroll,certificates,revoke,view}.go (+ service.go, store_sql.go changes)
internal/grpcsrv/{enroll,streams,servercert}.go (+ auth.go, server.go, events.go changes)
internal/worker/{identity,enroll,pin,renew}.go    (+ config.go, client.go, loop.go changes)
internal/db/migrations/00024_job_trigger_ref.sql
internal/db/migrations/00025_schedules.sql
internal/db/migrations/00026_job_triggers.sql
internal/db/migrations/00027_worker_identity.sql
internal/db/queries/{schedules,triggers}.sql      (+ jobs.sql, workers.sql additions)
proto/pulsix/worker/v1/worker.proto               + Enroll, RenewCertificate
internal/api/{schedule_handlers,schedule_dto,hook_handlers,trigger_handlers,worker_handlers,worker_snippets}.go
internal/app/{app.go,options.go,embedded_worker.go,workeradd.go,revoke_ports.go}
cmd/worker/main.go
web/src/api/hooks/{useSchedules,useTriggers,useWorkers}.ts
web/src/features/schedules/*   web/src/features/triggers/*   web/src/features/workers/*
internal/e2e/automation_test.go                   milestone E2E
deploy/compose/{docker-compose.yml,README.md,.env.example}, scripts/smoke.sh
```

---

### Task 1: Instance configuration and trusted-proxy client IP

Adds `PULSIX_SCHEDULE_TICK` and `PULSIX_TRUSTED_PROXIES`, the client-IP helper that the webhook endpoint, the enrollment limiter and the audit log share (spec §3 "Client IP"), and the per-address limiter that both unauthenticated write paths use (spec §9 "per-IP rate limits"). The audit middleware starts recording `meta.source_ip`.

**Files:**
- Create: `internal/clientip/clientip.go`, `internal/clientip/clientip_test.go`, `internal/clientip/limiter.go`, `internal/clientip/limiter_test.go`
- Modify: `internal/config/config.go` (two fields, two parse blocks), `internal/config/config_test.go`
- Modify: `internal/api/api.go` (`Deps.ClientIP`, `Deps.validate`), `internal/api/audit_middleware.go` (meta), `internal/api/apitest/apitest.go` (`ClientIP: clientip.New(nil)`), `internal/app/app.go` (`ClientIP: clientip.New(cfg.TrustedProxies)`)
- Modify: `scripts/coverage-gate.sh` (add `internal/clientip` to the 90 % list)
- Test: `internal/api/audit_source_ip_test.go`
- Docs (noix-docs): `admin/configuration/environment-variables.md` (`PULSIX_SCHEDULE_TICK`, `PULSIX_TRUSTED_PROXIES`)

**Interfaces:**
- Produces (package `clientip`):
  - `type Resolver struct{ /* trusted []netip.Prefix */ }`
  - `func New(trusted []netip.Prefix) *Resolver`
  - `func (r *Resolver) Resolve(remoteAddr string, forwardedFor []string) netip.Addr` (an invalid `netip.Addr{}` when the peer is unparsable)
  - `func (r *Resolver) FromRequest(req *http.Request) netip.Addr`
  - `func ParsePrefixes(raw string) ([]netip.Prefix, error)` (a comma-separated list of CIDRs or bare addresses; a bare address becomes /32 or /128)
  - `type Limiter struct{ /* fixed window per address */ }`, `func NewLimiter(limit int, per time.Duration, now func() time.Time) *Limiter`, `func (l *Limiter) Allow(a netip.Addr) bool`, `func (l *Limiter) Window() time.Duration`. Task 10 (webhook deliveries) and Task 13 (`Enroll`) use it.
- Produces (package `config`): `Config.ScheduleTick time.Duration` (default `DefaultScheduleTick = 15 * time.Second`, minimum `MinScheduleTick = 5 * time.Second`), `Config.TrustedProxies []netip.Prefix`.
- Produces (package `api`): `Deps.ClientIP *clientip.Resolver` (required), plus the unexported helper `func clientAddr(deps Deps, ctx huma.Context) netip.Addr` that later tasks use.

`10.0.0.1/8` is refused because a prefix with host bits set is almost always a typo (`netip.Prefix.Masked() != p`).

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement `internal/clientip/clientip.go`**

```go
// Package clientip resolves the address a request came from. Only when the
// TCP peer is a configured trusted proxy (PULSIX_TRUSTED_PROXIES) is
// X-Forwarded-For consulted; then the client is the right-most entry that
// is not itself a trusted proxy. Everything to the left of that entry is
// client-controlled and never trusted (spec 2026-09-18 §3 "Client IP").
package clientip

import (
	"fmt"
	"net"
	"net/http"
	"net/netip"
	"strings"
)

// Resolver holds the trusted proxy ranges. The zero value trusts nothing.
type Resolver struct {
	trusted []netip.Prefix
}

// New returns a Resolver trusting the given ranges.
func New(trusted []netip.Prefix) *Resolver {
	return &Resolver{trusted: append([]netip.Prefix(nil), trusted...)}
}

// Resolve returns the client address for a TCP peer (host:port or a bare
// address) and the X-Forwarded-For header lines in order of appearance.
// The result is invalid only when the peer itself is unparsable.
func (r *Resolver) Resolve(remoteAddr string, forwardedFor []string) netip.Addr {
	peer := parseAddr(remoteAddr)
	if !peer.IsValid() || !r.trustedAddr(peer) {
		return peer
	}
	var hops []string
	for _, line := range forwardedFor {
		for _, part := range strings.Split(line, ",") {
			if p := strings.TrimSpace(part); p != "" {
				hops = append(hops, p)
			}
		}
	}
	client := peer
	for i := len(hops) - 1; i >= 0; i-- {
		a := parseAddr(hops[i])
		if !a.IsValid() {
			// A malformed hop ends the walk: the nearest address seen so
			// far is the best non-spoofable answer.
			return client
		}
		client = a
		if !r.trustedAddr(a) {
			return a
		}
	}
	return client
}

// FromRequest resolves the client address of an HTTP request.
func (r *Resolver) FromRequest(req *http.Request) netip.Addr {
	return r.Resolve(req.RemoteAddr, req.Header.Values("X-Forwarded-For"))
}

func (r *Resolver) trustedAddr(a netip.Addr) bool {
	for _, p := range r.trusted {
		if p.Contains(a) {
			return true
		}
	}
	return false
}

// parseAddr accepts "ip", "ip:port" and "[ipv6]:port" and unmaps
// IPv4-mapped IPv6 addresses.
func parseAddr(s string) netip.Addr {
	s = strings.TrimSpace(s)
	if ap, err := netip.ParseAddrPort(s); err == nil {
		return ap.Addr().Unmap()
	}
	if host, _, err := net.SplitHostPort(s); err == nil {
		s = host
	}
	a, err := netip.ParseAddr(strings.TrimSuffix(strings.TrimPrefix(s, "["), "]"))
	if err != nil {
		return netip.Addr{}
	}
	return a.Unmap()
}

// ParsePrefixes parses a comma-separated list of CIDRs or bare addresses.
// A prefix with host bits set is refused as a likely typo.
func ParsePrefixes(raw string) ([]netip.Prefix, error) {
	var out []netip.Prefix
	for _, part := range strings.Split(raw, ",") {
		p := strings.TrimSpace(part)
		if p == "" {
			continue
		}
		if !strings.Contains(p, "/") {
			a, err := netip.ParseAddr(p)
			if err != nil {
				return nil, fmt.Errorf("%q is neither an address nor a CIDR", p)
			}
			out = append(out, netip.PrefixFrom(a.Unmap(), a.Unmap().BitLen()))
			continue
		}
		pre, err := netip.ParsePrefix(p)
		if err != nil {
			return nil, fmt.Errorf("%q is not a CIDR: %v", p, err)
		}
		if pre.Masked() != pre {
			return nil, fmt.Errorf("%q has host bits set; did you mean %s?", p, pre.Masked())
		}
		out = append(out, pre)
	}
	return out, nil
}
```

```go
package clientip_test

import (
	"net/netip"
	"testing"
	"time"

	"github.com/noixdev/pulsix/internal/clientip"
)

func TestLimiter(t *testing.T) {
	now := time.Date(2026, 9, 18, 10, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC)
	l := clientip.NewLimiter(2, time.Minute, func() time.Time { return now })
	a, b := netip.MustParseAddr("198.51.100.1"), netip.MustParseAddr("198.51.100.2")
	got := []bool{l.Allow(a), l.Allow(a), l.Allow(a)}
	if !got[0] || !got[1] || got[2] {
		t.Fatalf("calls inside one window = %v, want the third refused", got)
	}
	if !l.Allow(b) {
		t.Fatal("addresses are counted separately")
	}
	now = now.Add(time.Minute)
	if !l.Allow(a) {
		t.Fatal("a new window starts after the old one ends")
	}
	if l.Window() != time.Minute {
		t.Fatalf("window = %s", l.Window())
	}
}

func TestLimiterDropsExpiredWindowsWhenFull(t *testing.T) {
	now := time.Date(2026, 9, 18, 10, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC)
	l := clientip.NewLimiter(1, time.Minute, func() time.Time { return now })
	for i := 0; i < 10000; i++ {
		l.Allow(netip.AddrFrom4([4]byte{10, byte(i >> 16), byte(i >> 8), byte(i)}))
	}
	now = now.Add(time.Minute)
	first := netip.AddrFrom4([4]byte{10, 0, 0, 0})
	if !l.Allow(first) {
		t.Fatal("an expired window must not block the address")
	}
	if !l.Allow(netip.MustParseAddr("192.0.2.1")) {
		t.Fatal("a new address is counted once the table was pruned")
	}
}

func TestNewLimiterDefaultsTheClock(t *testing.T) {
	if !clientip.NewLimiter(1, time.Minute, nil).Allow(netip.MustParseAddr("192.0.2.1")) {
		t.Fatal("the first call is always allowed")
	}
}
```

Expected: FAIL, `undefined: clientip.NewLimiter`.

`internal/clientip/limiter.go`:

```go
package clientip

import (
	"net/netip"
	"sync"
	"time"
)

// maxTracked bounds the limiter's memory: once reached, expired windows
// are dropped before a new address is counted.
const maxTracked = 10000

// Limiter is a fixed-window call counter per client address, in process.
// The unauthenticated write paths use it (webhook deliveries and worker
// enrollment) so one source cannot flood them. It is per manager: with
// several managers, each counts on its own.
type Limiter struct {
	mu     sync.Mutex
	now    func() time.Time
	limit  int
	window time.Duration
	seen   map[netip.Addr]*window
}

type window struct {
	start time.Time
	count int
}

// NewLimiter allows limit calls per address and window.
func NewLimiter(limit int, per time.Duration, now func() time.Time) *Limiter {
	if now == nil {
		now = time.Now
	}
	return &Limiter{now: now, limit: limit, window: per, seen: map[netip.Addr]*window{}}
}

// Window is the length of one counting window (the Retry-After bound).
func (l *Limiter) Window() time.Duration { return l.window }

// Allow counts one call from a and reports whether it is within the limit.
func (l *Limiter) Allow(a netip.Addr) bool {
	l.mu.Lock()
	defer l.mu.Unlock()
	now := l.now()
	if len(l.seen) >= maxTracked {
		for k, w := range l.seen {
			if now.Sub(w.start) >= l.window {
				delete(l.seen, k)
			}
		}
	}
	w, ok := l.seen[a]
	if !ok || now.Sub(w.start) >= l.window {
		l.seen[a] = &window{start: now, count: 1}
		return true
	}
	if w.count >= l.limit {
		return false
	}
	w.count++
	return true
}
```

```go
// TestScheduleTick: PULSIX_SCHEDULE_TICK drives the schedule ticker; the
// default is 15s and anything below 5s is refused (spec §2 "Ticker").
func TestScheduleTick(t *testing.T) {
	base := map[string]string{
		"PULSIX_DATABASE_URL": "postgres://u:p@db/pulsix",
		"PULSIX_MASTER_KEY":   strings.Repeat("ab", 32),
		"PULSIX_PUBLIC_URL":   "https://pulsix.example.test",
	}
	cases := []struct {
		raw     string
		want    time.Duration
		wantErr bool
	}{
		{raw: "", want: 15 * time.Second},
		{raw: "30s", want: 30 * time.Second},
		{raw: "5s", want: 5 * time.Second},
		{raw: "4s", wantErr: true},
		{raw: "0", wantErr: true},
		{raw: "soon", wantErr: true},
	}
	for _, c := range cases {
		m := map[string]string{"PULSIX_SCHEDULE_TICK": c.raw}
		for k, v := range base {
			m[k] = v
		}
		cfg, err := config.Load(env(m))
		if c.wantErr {
			if err == nil {
				t.Errorf("PULSIX_SCHEDULE_TICK=%q: expected an error", c.raw)
			}
			continue
		}
		if err != nil || cfg.ScheduleTick != c.want {
			t.Errorf("PULSIX_SCHEDULE_TICK=%q: got %v, %v; want %v", c.raw, cfg.ScheduleTick, err, c.want)
		}
	}
}

	if _, err := config.Load(env(base)); err == nil {
		t.Fatal("an invalid CIDR must be refused")
	}
}
```

```go
// clientAddr resolves the request's client address through the trusted
// proxy list (internal/clientip). huma exposes headers one callback at a
// time, so X-Forwarded-For lines are collected in order first.
func clientAddr(deps Deps, ctx huma.Context) netip.Addr {
	var xff []string
	ctx.EachHeader(func(name, value string) {
		if strings.EqualFold(name, "X-Forwarded-For") {
			xff = append(xff, value)
		}
	})
	return deps.ClientIP.Resolve(ctx.RemoteAddr(), xff)
}
```

### Task 2: The cron evaluator (`internal/cronexpr`)

This is the shared evaluator for schedules and, in v0.2d, maintenance windows. It is pure code: parsing, DST-correct `Next`, simple-mode compile and human text.

**Files:**
- Create: `internal/cronexpr/parse.go`, `next.go`, `simple.go`
- Test: `internal/cronexpr/parse_test.go`, `next_test.go`, `simple_test.go`

**Interfaces:**
- Produces (package `cronexpr`):
  - `var ErrSyntax = errors.New("invalid cron expression")`
  - `type Expr struct{ /* bitsets, star flags, canonical spec */ }`
  - `func Parse(spec string) (Expr, error)`
  - `func (e Expr) String() string`: the canonical spec (a macro stays a macro, and fields are single-space joined)
  - `func (e Expr) Next(after time.Time, loc *time.Location) time.Time`: the first occurrence strictly after `after`, or zero when there is none within 5 years
  - `func (e Expr) NextN(after time.Time, loc *time.Location, n int) []time.Time`
  - `func (e Expr) Between(from, to time.Time, loc *time.Location, max int) (count int, latest time.Time)`: the occurrences in `[from, to]`, counted up to `max`
  - `type Simple struct { Kind string; Every int; Time string; Days []string; Day int }` with JSON tags `kind`, `every,omitempty`, `time,omitempty`, `days,omitempty`, `day,omitempty`
  - Kind constants: `SimpleEveryMinutes = "every_minutes"`, `SimpleEveryHours = "every_hours"`, `SimpleDaily = "daily"`, `SimpleWeekly = "weekly"`, `SimpleMonthly = "monthly"`
  - `func (s Simple) Compile() (string, error)` and `func (s Simple) Describe() string`
  - `func FromCron(spec string) (Simple, bool)` and `func Describe(spec string) string`

`internal/cronexpr/parse_test.go`:

```go
package cronexpr_test

import (
	"errors"
	"testing"

	"github.com/noixdev/pulsix/internal/cronexpr"
)

func TestParseAccepts(t *testing.T) {
	cases := map[string]string{
		"0 3 * * 2,4":          "0 3 * * 2,4",
		" 0   3 * *  2,4 ":     "0 3 * * 2,4",
		"*/15 * * * *":         "*/15 * * * *",
		"0 9-17/2 * * MON-FRI": "0 9-17/2 * * MON-FRI",
		"0 0 1 jan,Jul *":      "0 0 1 jan,Jul *",
		"0 0 * * 7":            "0 0 * * 7",
		"0 0 ? * sun":          "0 0 ? * sun",
		"5/10 * * * *":         "5/10 * * * *",
		"@hourly":              "@hourly",
		"@DAILY":               "@daily",
		"@weekly":              "@weekly",
		"@monthly":             "@monthly",
	}
	for in, want := range cases {
		e, err := cronexpr.Parse(in)
		if err != nil {
			t.Errorf("Parse(%q): %v", in, err)
			continue
		}
		if e.String() != want {
			t.Errorf("Parse(%q).String() = %q, want %q", in, e.String(), want)
		}
	}
}

func TestParseRejects(t *testing.T) {
	for _, in := range []string{
		"", "* * * *", "* * * * * *", "60 * * * *", "* 24 * * *", "* * 0 * *",
		"* * 32 * *", "* * * 13 *", "* * * * 8", "5-1 * * * *", "*/0 * * * *",
		"*/61 * * * *", "1,,2 * * * *", "a * * * *", "* * * foo *", "@yearly",
		"@every 5m", "@reboot", "CRON_TZ=UTC 0 * * * *", "0 3 L * *", "0 3 * * 5#2",
	} {
		if _, err := cronexpr.Parse(in); !errors.Is(err, cronexpr.ErrSyntax) {
			t.Errorf("Parse(%q) = %v, want ErrSyntax", in, err)
		}
	}
}
```

`internal/cronexpr/next_test.go`:

```go
package cronexpr_test

import (
	"testing"
	"time"

	"github.com/noixdev/pulsix/internal/cronexpr"
)

func loc(t *testing.T, name string) *time.Location {
	t.Helper()
	l, err := time.LoadLocation(name)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	return l
}

func mustParse(t *testing.T, spec string) cronexpr.Expr {
	t.Helper()
	e, err := cronexpr.Parse(spec)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	return e
}

// fmtAll renders instants in RFC 3339 with their zone offset, so a test
// failure shows which of two same-wall-clock instants was chosen.
func fmtAll(ts []time.Time, l *time.Location) []string {
	out := make([]string, len(ts))
	for i, t := range ts {
		out[i] = t.In(l).Format(time.RFC3339)
	}
	return out
}

func TestNextN(t *testing.T) {
	berlin := loc(t, "Europe/Berlin")
	tokyo := loc(t, "Asia/Tokyo")
	cases := []struct {
		name string
		spec string
		loc  *time.Location
		from time.Time
		want []string
	}{
		{
			// 2026-03-29 02:00 CET jumps to 03:00 CEST: 02:30 does not exist
			// and runs at the first valid minute after the gap.
			name: "spring forward runs at the first valid minute",
			spec: "30 2 * * *", loc: berlin,
			from: time.Date(2026, 3, 28, 12, 0, 0, 0, berlin),
			want: []string{"2026-03-29T03:00:00+02:00", "2026-03-30T02:30:00+02:00"},
		},
		{
			name: "spring forward collapses a whole skipped hour into one run",
			spec: "*/15 * * * *", loc: berlin,
			from: time.Date(2026, 3, 29, 1, 50, 0, 0, berlin),
			want: []string{"2026-03-29T03:00:00+02:00", "2026-03-29T03:15:00+02:00"},
		},
		{
			// 2026-10-25 03:00 CEST falls back to 02:00 CET: 02:30 happens
			// twice and runs once, at its first occurrence.
			name: "fall back runs once at the first occurrence",
			spec: "30 2 * * *", loc: berlin,
			from: time.Date(2026, 10, 24, 12, 0, 0, 0, berlin),
			want: []string{"2026-10-25T02:30:00+02:00", "2026-10-26T02:30:00+01:00"},
		},
		{
			name: "fall back does not replay the repeated hour",
			spec: "*/30 * * * *", loc: berlin,
			from: time.Date(2026, 10, 25, 1, 50, 0, 0, berlin),
			want: []string{"2026-10-25T02:00:00+02:00", "2026-10-25T02:30:00+02:00", "2026-10-25T03:00:00+01:00"},
		},
		{
			name: "day of month OR day of week when both are restricted",
			spec: "0 0 15 * FRI", loc: berlin,
			from: time.Date(2026, 9, 1, 0, 0, 0, 0, berlin),
			want: []string{"2026-09-04T00:00:00+02:00", "2026-09-11T00:00:00+02:00", "2026-09-15T00:00:00+02:00", "2026-09-18T00:00:00+02:00"},
		},
		{
			name: "a star-prefixed day of month leaves day of week alone",
			spec: "0 0 */2 * MON", loc: time.UTC,
			from: time.Date(2026, 9, 1, 0, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC),
			want: []string{"2026-09-07T00:00:00Z", "2026-09-14T00:00:00Z"},
		},
		{
			name: "sunday as 7",
			spec: "0 0 * * 7", loc: time.UTC,
			from: time.Date(2026, 9, 1, 0, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC),
			want: []string{"2026-09-06T00:00:00Z"},
		},
		{
			name: "weekly macro",
			spec: "@weekly", loc: berlin,
			from: time.Date(2026, 9, 1, 0, 0, 0, 0, berlin),
			want: []string{"2026-09-06T00:00:00+02:00", "2026-09-13T00:00:00+02:00"},
		},
		{
			name: "time zone",
			spec: "0 9 * * 1-5", loc: tokyo,
			from: time.Date(2026, 9, 17, 23, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC), // Fri 08:00 JST
			want: []string{"2026-09-18T09:00:00+09:00", "2026-09-21T09:00:00+09:00"},
		},
		{
			name: "strictly after: an exact match of the reference is skipped",
			spec: "0 * * * *", loc: time.UTC,
			from: time.Date(2026, 9, 18, 10, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC),
			want: []string{"2026-09-18T11:00:00Z"},
		},
		{
			name: "seconds of the reference are ignored",
			spec: "* * * * *", loc: time.UTC,
			from: time.Date(2026, 9, 18, 10, 0, 59, 999, time.UTC),
			want: []string{"2026-09-18T10:01:00Z"},
		},
		{
			name: "leap day",
			spec: "0 0 29 2 *", loc: time.UTC,
			from: time.Date(2026, 1, 1, 0, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC),
			want: []string{"2028-02-29T00:00:00Z"},
		},
	}
	for _, c := range cases {
		t.Run(c.name, func(t *testing.T) {
			got := fmtAll(mustParse(t, c.spec).NextN(c.from, c.loc, len(c.want)), c.loc)
			for i := range c.want {
				if i >= len(got) || got[i] != c.want[i] {
					t.Fatalf("NextN = %v, want %v", got, c.want)
				}
			}
		})
	}
}

func TestNextNeverFires(t *testing.T) {
	if got := mustParse(t, "0 0 30 2 *").Next(time.Now(), time.UTC); !got.IsZero() {
		t.Fatalf("Feb 30 fired at %v", got)
	}
	if got := mustParse(t, "0 0 30 2 *").NextN(time.Now(), time.UTC, 3); len(got) != 0 {
		t.Fatalf("NextN = %v", got)
	}
}

func TestNextNilLocationIsUTC(t *testing.T) {
	got := mustParse(t, "0 12 * * *").Next(time.Date(2026, 9, 18, 0, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC), nil)
	if got.Format(time.RFC3339) != "2026-09-18T12:00:00Z" {
		t.Fatalf("got %v", got)
	}
}

func TestBetween(t *testing.T) {
	e := mustParse(t, "*/10 * * * *")
	from := time.Date(2026, 9, 18, 10, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC)
	count, latest := e.Between(from, from.Add(35*time.Minute), time.UTC, 1000)
	if count != 4 || !latest.Equal(from.Add(30*time.Minute)) {
		t.Fatalf("Between = %d, %v", count, latest)
	}
	count, _ = e.Between(from, from.Add(24*time.Hour), time.UTC, 5)
	if count != 5 {
		t.Fatalf("capped count = %d, want 5", count)
	}
	if count, latest := e.Between(from.Add(time.Minute), from.Add(2*time.Minute), time.UTC, 10); count != 0 || !latest.IsZero() {
		t.Fatalf("empty window = %d, %v", count, latest)
	}
}
```

```go
package cronexpr_test

import (
	"testing"

	"github.com/noixdev/pulsix/internal/cronexpr"
)

func TestSimpleCompileAndDescribe(t *testing.T) {
	cases := []struct {
		in       cronexpr.Simple
		cron     string
		describe string
	}{
		{cronexpr.Simple{Kind: "every_minutes", Every: 1}, "* * * * *", "every minute"},
		{cronexpr.Simple{Kind: "every_minutes", Every: 30}, "*/30 * * * *", "every 30 min"},
		{cronexpr.Simple{Kind: "every_hours", Every: 1}, "0 * * * *", "every hour"},
		{cronexpr.Simple{Kind: "every_hours", Every: 6}, "0 */6 * * *", "every 6 h"},
		{cronexpr.Simple{Kind: "daily", Time: "03:00"}, "0 3 * * *", "daily at 03:00"},
		{cronexpr.Simple{Kind: "weekly", Days: []string{"thu", "tue"}, Time: "03:00"}, "0 3 * * 2,4", "Tue, Thu at 03:00"},
		{cronexpr.Simple{Kind: "weekly", Days: []string{"sun"}, Time: "23:45"}, "45 23 * * 0", "Sun at 23:45"},
		{cronexpr.Simple{Kind: "monthly", Day: 1, Time: "04:00"}, "0 4 1 * *", "monthly on day 1 at 04:00"},
	}
	for _, c := range cases {
		got, err := c.in.Compile()
		if err != nil || got != c.cron {
			t.Errorf("Compile(%+v) = %q, %v; want %q", c.in, got, err, c.cron)
		}
		if d := c.in.Describe(); d != c.describe {
			t.Errorf("Describe(%+v) = %q, want %q", c.in, d, c.describe)
		}
		// The compiled form round-trips to the same description.
		if d := cronexpr.Describe(got); d != c.describe {
			t.Errorf("Describe(%q) = %q, want %q", got, d, c.describe)
		}
	}
}

func TestSimpleCompileRejects(t *testing.T) {
	for _, in := range []cronexpr.Simple{
		{Kind: "every_minutes", Every: 7},
		{Kind: "every_minutes", Every: 0},
		{Kind: "every_hours", Every: 5},
		{Kind: "daily", Time: "24:00"},
		{Kind: "daily", Time: "3:00"},
		{Kind: "daily"},
		{Kind: "weekly", Time: "03:00"},
		{Kind: "weekly", Days: []string{"funday"}, Time: "03:00"},
		{Kind: "weekly", Days: []string{"mon", "mon"}, Time: "03:00"},
		{Kind: "monthly", Day: 0, Time: "03:00"},
		{Kind: "monthly", Day: 32, Time: "03:00"},
		{Kind: "yearly"},
	} {
		if _, err := in.Compile(); err == nil {
			t.Errorf("Compile(%+v) accepted", in)
		}
	}
}

func TestDescribeUnknownShape(t *testing.T) {
	for _, spec := range []string{"0 9-17 * * 1-5", "5 4 * 1 *", "garbage"} {
		if d := cronexpr.Describe(spec); d != "" {
			t.Errorf("Describe(%q) = %q, want empty", spec, d)
		}
	}
	if d := cronexpr.Describe("@daily"); d != "daily at 00:00" {
		t.Errorf("Describe(@daily) = %q", d)
	}
}
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Implement `parse.go`**

```go
// Package cronexpr evaluates 5-field cron expressions in an IANA time zone
// with the DST rules of spec 2026-09-18 §2: a wall time skipped by a
// spring-forward runs at the first valid minute after the gap, a wall time
// repeated by a fall-back runs once, at its first occurrence. Day of month
// and day of week combine with OR when both are restricted (Vixie cron).
package cronexpr

import (
	"errors"
	"fmt"
	"strconv"
	"strings"
)

// ErrSyntax wraps every parse failure.
var ErrSyntax = errors.New("invalid cron expression")

// Expr is a parsed expression. Bit v of a field set means value v matches.
type Expr struct {
	minute, hour, dom, month, dow uint64
	domStar, dowStar              bool
	spec                          string
}

var macros = map[string]string{
	"@hourly":  "0 * * * *",
	"@daily":   "0 0 * * *",
	"@weekly":  "0 0 * * 0",
	"@monthly": "0 0 1 * *",
}

type field struct {
	name     string
	min, max int
	names    map[string]int
}

var (
	minuteField = field{name: "minute", min: 0, max: 59}
	hourField   = field{name: "hour", min: 0, max: 23}
	domField    = field{name: "day of month", min: 1, max: 31}
	monthField  = field{name: "month", min: 1, max: 12, names: map[string]int{
		"jan": 1, "feb": 2, "mar": 3, "apr": 4, "may": 5, "jun": 6,
		"jul": 7, "aug": 8, "sep": 9, "oct": 10, "nov": 11, "dec": 12,
	}}
	dowField = field{name: "day of week", min: 0, max: 7, names: map[string]int{
		"sun": 0, "mon": 1, "tue": 2, "wed": 3, "thu": 4, "fri": 5, "sat": 6,
	}}
)

// Parse parses a 5-field expression or one of @hourly, @daily, @weekly,
// @monthly.
func Parse(spec string) (Expr, error) {
	s := strings.TrimSpace(spec)
	if strings.HasPrefix(s, "@") {
		m, ok := macros[strings.ToLower(s)]
		if !ok {
			return Expr{}, fmt.Errorf("%w: unsupported macro %q (use @hourly, @daily, @weekly or @monthly)", ErrSyntax, s)
		}
		e, err := Parse(m)
		e.spec = strings.ToLower(s)
		return e, err
	}
	parts := strings.Fields(s)
	if len(parts) != 5 {
		return Expr{}, fmt.Errorf("%w: want 5 fields (minute hour day-of-month month day-of-week), got %d", ErrSyntax, len(parts))
	}
	var e Expr
	var err error
	if e.minute, _, err = parseField(parts[0], minuteField); err != nil {
		return Expr{}, err
	}
	if e.hour, _, err = parseField(parts[1], hourField); err != nil {
		return Expr{}, err
	}
	if e.dom, e.domStar, err = parseField(parts[2], domField); err != nil {
		return Expr{}, err
	}
	if e.month, _, err = parseField(parts[3], monthField); err != nil {
		return Expr{}, err
	}
	if e.dow, e.dowStar, err = parseField(parts[4], dowField); err != nil {
		return Expr{}, err
	}
	if e.dow&(1<<7) != 0 { // 7 is Sunday
		e.dow = e.dow&^(1<<7) | 1
	}
	e.spec = strings.Join(parts, " ")
	return e, nil
}

// String returns the canonical form of the expression.
func (e Expr) String() string { return e.spec }

func parseField(raw string, f field) (bits uint64, star bool, err error) {
	star = strings.HasPrefix(raw, "*") || raw == "?"
	if raw == "?" {
		raw = "*"
	}
	for _, item := range strings.Split(raw, ",") {
		b, err := parseItem(item, f)
		if err != nil {
			return 0, false, err
		}
		bits |= b
	}
	return bits, star, nil
}

func parseItem(item string, f field) (uint64, error) {
	rng, stepRaw, hasStep := strings.Cut(item, "/")
	step := 1
	if hasStep {
		n, err := strconv.Atoi(stepRaw)
		if err != nil || n < 1 || n > f.max {
			return 0, fmt.Errorf("%w: %s step %q", ErrSyntax, f.name, stepRaw)
		}
		step = n
	}
	lo, hi := f.min, f.max
	switch {
	case rng == "*":
	case strings.Contains(rng, "-"):
		a, b, _ := strings.Cut(rng, "-")
		var err error
		if lo, err = f.value(a); err != nil {
			return 0, err
		}
		if hi, err = f.value(b); err != nil {
			return 0, err
		}
		if lo > hi {
			return 0, fmt.Errorf("%w: %s range %q runs backwards", ErrSyntax, f.name, rng)
		}
	default:
		v, err := f.value(rng)
		if err != nil {
			return 0, err
		}
		lo = v
		if !hasStep {
			hi = v
		}
	}
	return span(lo, hi, step), nil
}

func (f field) value(s string) (int, error) {
	if n, ok := f.names[strings.ToLower(s)]; ok {
		return n, nil
	}
	n, err := strconv.Atoi(s)
	if err != nil || n < f.min || n > f.max {
		return 0, fmt.Errorf("%w: %s value %q (allowed %d-%d)", ErrSyntax, f.name, s, f.min, f.max)
	}
	return n, nil
}

func span(lo, hi, step int) uint64 {
	var b uint64
	for v := lo; v <= hi; v += step {
		b |= 1 << uint(v)
	}
	return b
}

func has(bits uint64, v int) bool { return bits&(1<<uint(v)) != 0 }
```

- [ ] **Step 6: Implement `next.go`**

```go
package cronexpr

import "time"

// searchYears bounds Next: an expression with no occurrence in five years
// (Feb 30) never fires. Five years always include a Feb 29.
const searchYears = 5

// Next returns the first occurrence strictly after `after`, evaluated in
// loc (nil is UTC), or the zero time when there is none within five years.
// The walk runs over wall-clock minutes (carried in UTC fields, so no DST
// arithmetic distorts it) and maps each match to an instant with instant().
func (e Expr) Next(after time.Time, loc *time.Location) time.Time {
	if loc == nil {
		loc = time.UTC
	}
	a := after.In(loc)
	w := time.Date(a.Year(), a.Month(), a.Day(), a.Hour(), a.Minute(), 0, 0, time.UTC).Add(time.Minute)
	end := w.AddDate(searchYears, 0, 0)
	for w.Before(end) {
		switch {
		case !has(e.month, int(w.Month())):
			w = time.Date(w.Year(), w.Month()+1, 1, 0, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC)
		case !e.dayMatches(w):
			w = time.Date(w.Year(), w.Month(), w.Day()+1, 0, 0, 0, 0, time.UTC)
		case !has(e.hour, w.Hour()):
			w = w.Truncate(time.Hour).Add(time.Hour)
		case !has(e.minute, w.Minute()):
			w = w.Add(time.Minute)
		default:
			if t := instant(w, loc); t.After(after) {
				return t
			}
			w = w.Add(time.Minute)
		}
	}
	return time.Time{}
}

// NextN returns up to n successive occurrences after `after`.
func (e Expr) NextN(after time.Time, loc *time.Location, n int) []time.Time {
	out := make([]time.Time, 0, n)
	t := after
	for len(out) < n {
		t = e.Next(t, loc)
		if t.IsZero() {
			break
		}
		out = append(out, t)
	}
	return out
}

// Between counts the occurrences in [from, to] (up to max) and returns the
// latest one counted.
func (e Expr) Between(from, to time.Time, loc *time.Location, max int) (count int, latest time.Time) {
	t := from.Add(-time.Nanosecond)
	for count < max {
		t = e.Next(t, loc)
		if t.IsZero() || t.After(to) {
			break
		}
		count, latest = count+1, t
	}
	return count, latest
}

func (e Expr) dayMatches(w time.Time) bool {
	dom, dow := has(e.dom, w.Day()), has(e.dow, int(w.Weekday()))
	switch {
	case e.domStar && e.dowStar:
		return true
	case e.domStar:
		return dow
	case e.dowStar:
		return dom
	default:
		return dom || dow
	}
}

// instant maps a wall-clock minute w (in UTC fields) to the instant it
// names in loc. A wall time inside a spring-forward gap runs at the first
// valid minute after the gap: the start of the zone period time.Date
// normalized into. A wall time repeated by a fall-back resolves to its
// first (earlier) instant; time.Date's own choice is unspecified.
func instant(w time.Time, loc *time.Location) time.Time {
	t := time.Date(w.Year(), w.Month(), w.Day(), w.Hour(), w.Minute(), 0, 0, loc)
	if !sameWall(t, w) {
		start, _ := t.ZoneBounds()
		return start
	}
	first := t
	for _, back := range []time.Duration{30 * time.Minute, time.Hour, 2 * time.Hour} {
		if u := t.Add(-back); sameWall(u, w) {
			first = u
		}
	}
	return first
}

func sameWall(t, w time.Time) bool {
	return t.Year() == w.Year() && t.Month() == w.Month() && t.Day() == w.Day() &&
		t.Hour() == w.Hour() && t.Minute() == w.Minute()
}
```

- [ ] **Step 7: Implement `simple.go`**

```go
package cronexpr

import (
	"fmt"
	"regexp"
	"slices"
	"strconv"
	"strings"
)

// Simple-mode kinds (spec §2): the form's input, compiled to cron on save.
const (
	SimpleEveryMinutes = "every_minutes"
	SimpleEveryHours   = "every_hours"
	SimpleDaily        = "daily"
	SimpleWeekly       = "weekly"
	SimpleMonthly      = "monthly"
)

// Simple is the simple-mode form input, stored verbatim so the form
// round-trips.
type Simple struct {
	Kind  string   `json:"kind"`
	Every int      `json:"every,omitempty"`
	Time  string   `json:"time,omitempty"`
	Days  []string `json:"days,omitempty"`
	Day   int      `json:"day,omitempty"`
}

var (
	minuteSteps = []int{1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 10, 12, 15, 20, 30}
	hourSteps   = []int{1, 2, 3, 4, 6, 8, 12}
	dayOrder    = []string{"mon", "tue", "wed", "thu", "fri", "sat", "sun"}
	dayNumber   = map[string]int{"sun": 0, "mon": 1, "tue": 2, "wed": 3, "thu": 4, "fri": 5, "sat": 6}
	timeRE      = regexp.MustCompile(`^([01]\d|2[0-3]):([0-5]\d)$`)
)

// Compile turns the form input into a cron expression.
func (s Simple) Compile() (string, error) {
	switch s.Kind {
	case SimpleEveryMinutes:
		if !slices.Contains(minuteSteps, s.Every) {
			return "", fmt.Errorf("%w: every must be one of %v minutes", ErrSyntax, minuteSteps)
		}
		if s.Every == 1 {
			return "* * * * *", nil
		}
		return fmt.Sprintf("*/%d * * * *", s.Every), nil
	case SimpleEveryHours:
		if !slices.Contains(hourSteps, s.Every) {
			return "", fmt.Errorf("%w: every must be one of %v hours", ErrSyntax, hourSteps)
		}
		if s.Every == 1 {
			return "0 * * * *", nil
		}
		return fmt.Sprintf("0 */%d * * *", s.Every), nil
	}
	h, m, err := clock(s.Time)
	if err != nil {
		return "", err
	}
	switch s.Kind {
	case SimpleDaily:
		return fmt.Sprintf("%d %d * * *", m, h), nil
	case SimpleWeekly:
		nums, err := weekdays(s.Days)
		if err != nil {
			return "", err
		}
		return fmt.Sprintf("%d %d * * %s", m, h, nums), nil
	case SimpleMonthly:
		if s.Day < 1 || s.Day > 31 {
			return "", fmt.Errorf("%w: day must be 1-31", ErrSyntax)
		}
		return fmt.Sprintf("%d %d %d * *", m, h, s.Day), nil
	}
	return "", fmt.Errorf("%w: unknown simple kind %q", ErrSyntax, s.Kind)
}

// Describe renders the input as the short text the schedules list shows.
func (s Simple) Describe() string {
	switch s.Kind {
	case SimpleEveryMinutes:
		if s.Every == 1 {
			return "every minute"
		}
		return fmt.Sprintf("every %d min", s.Every)
	case SimpleEveryHours:
		if s.Every == 1 {
			return "every hour"
		}
		return fmt.Sprintf("every %d h", s.Every)
	case SimpleDaily:
		return "daily at " + s.Time
	case SimpleWeekly:
		names := make([]string, 0, len(s.Days))
		for _, d := range dayOrder {
			if slices.Contains(s.Days, d) {
				names = append(names, strings.ToUpper(d[:1])+d[1:])
			}
		}
		return strings.Join(names, ", ") + " at " + s.Time
	case SimpleMonthly:
		return fmt.Sprintf("monthly on day %d at %s", s.Day, s.Time)
	}
	return ""
}

var (
	everyMinRE = regexp.MustCompile(`^(\*|\*/(\d+)) \* \* \* \*$`)
	everyHrRE  = regexp.MustCompile(`^0 (\*|\*/(\d+)) \* \* \*$`)
	atRE       = regexp.MustCompile(`^(\d{1,2}) (\d{1,2}) (\*|\d{1,2}) \* (\*|[0-6](?:,[0-6])*)$`)
)

// FromCron recognizes the expressions simple mode produces (and the macros).
func FromCron(spec string) (Simple, bool) {
	e, err := Parse(spec)
	if err != nil {
		return Simple{}, false
	}
	s := e.spec
	if m, ok := macros[s]; ok {
		s = m
	}
	if m := everyMinRE.FindStringSubmatch(s); m != nil {
		n := 1
		if m[2] != "" {
			n, _ = strconv.Atoi(m[2])
		}
		out := Simple{Kind: SimpleEveryMinutes, Every: n}
		_, cerr := out.Compile()
		return out, cerr == nil
	}
	if m := everyHrRE.FindStringSubmatch(s); m != nil {
		n := 1
		if m[2] != "" {
			n, _ = strconv.Atoi(m[2])
		}
		out := Simple{Kind: SimpleEveryHours, Every: n}
		_, cerr := out.Compile()
		return out, cerr == nil
	}
	m := atRE.FindStringSubmatch(s)
	if m == nil {
		return Simple{}, false
	}
	minute, _ := strconv.Atoi(m[1])
	hour, _ := strconv.Atoi(m[2])
	at := fmt.Sprintf("%02d:%02d", hour, minute)
	switch {
	case m[3] == "*" && m[4] == "*":
		return Simple{Kind: SimpleDaily, Time: at}, true
	case m[3] == "*":
		var days []string
		for _, n := range strings.Split(m[4], ",") {
			v, _ := strconv.Atoi(n)
			for name, num := range dayNumber {
				if num == v {
					days = append(days, name)
				}
			}
		}
		return Simple{Kind: SimpleWeekly, Days: days, Time: at}, true
	case m[4] == "*":
		d, _ := strconv.Atoi(m[3])
		return Simple{Kind: SimpleMonthly, Day: d, Time: at}, true
	}
	return Simple{}, false
}

// Describe returns human text for a cron expression of a simple shape and
// "" for everything else (the UI then shows the expression alone).
func Describe(spec string) string {
	s, ok := FromCron(spec)
	if !ok {
		return ""
	}
	return s.Describe()
}

func clock(v string) (hour, minute int, err error) {
	m := timeRE.FindStringSubmatch(v)
	if m == nil {
		return 0, 0, fmt.Errorf("%w: time must be HH:MM (00:00-23:59)", ErrSyntax)
	}
	hour, _ = strconv.Atoi(m[1])
	minute, _ = strconv.Atoi(m[2])
	return hour, minute, nil
}

func weekdays(days []string) (string, error) {
	if len(days) == 0 {
		return "", fmt.Errorf("%w: pick at least one day", ErrSyntax)
	}
	seen := map[string]bool{}
	for _, d := range days {
		if _, ok := dayNumber[d]; !ok || seen[d] {
			return "", fmt.Errorf("%w: days must be distinct values of mon..sun", ErrSyntax)
		}
		seen[d] = true
	}
	var nums []string
	for _, d := range []string{"sun", "mon", "tue", "wed", "thu", "fri", "sat"} {
		if seen[d] {
			nums = append(nums, strconv.Itoa(dayNumber[d]))
		}
	}
	return strings.Join(nums, ","), nil
}
```

### Task 3: Template run overrides (`internal/templates`)

Schedules store `limit`/`extra_vars` overrides and webhooks map them from payloads. Both must be "validated exactly like a manual run" (spec §2, §3). This task gives the templates service the one validation path both use.

**Files:**
- Create: `internal/templates/overrides.go`
- Modify: `internal/templates/service.go`. Extract `validateLimit(limit string) error` from `validateMeta` (which now calls it), and split `sealExtraVars` into `sealSecrets` (the MapSecrets pass, no validation) plus the existing `schema.Validate` call. Behaviour is unchanged for `Create`/`Update`.
- Test: `internal/templates/overrides_test.go`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `schema.MapSecrets`, `schema.Validate`, `schema.SealedBytes`, `ansiblevars.ForbiddenIn`, the existing `Store.Get` (unmasked values).
- Produces (package `templates`):

```go
// Overrides replaces a template's limit and top-level extra vars for one run.
type Overrides struct {
	Limit     *string        `json:"limit,omitempty"`
	ExtraVars map[string]any `json:"extra_vars,omitempty"`
}
func (o Overrides) Empty() bool

// OverrideOptions selects how secret-typed values are treated.
type OverrideOptions struct {
	SealAAD       []byte         // non-nil: seal plaintext secrets under this AAD (storage form)
	Stored        map[string]any // stored override vars that keep/mask markers resolve against
	RefuseSecrets bool           // webhook mapping: any secret-typed value is a problem
}

func (s *Service) ValidateOverrides(ctx context.Context, orgID, templateID uuid.UUID, in Overrides, opt OverrideOptions) (Overrides, error)
func (s *Service) RevealOverrides(ctx context.Context, orgID, templateID uuid.UUID, stored Overrides, aad []byte) (Overrides, error)
func SecretFields(sc *schema.Schema) map[string]bool // top-level names whose field is, or contains, a secret
```

Errors: `ErrNotFound` (template), `ErrInvalidInput` (limit), `*schema.ValidationError` (vars; paths are the var paths such as `region` or `vhosts[1].domain`).

```go
func strptr(s string) *string { return &s }

// overrideFixture stores a template with a schema: region (required string,
// satisfied by the template), replicas (int), token (secret, stored sealed).
func overrideFixture(t *testing.T) (fixture, templates.Template) {
	t.Helper()
	f := newFixture()
	sc := &schema.Schema{Fields: []schema.Field{
		{Name: "region", Type: schema.TypeString, Required: true},
		{Name: "replicas", Type: schema.TypeInt},

	})

		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	return f, tmpl
}

func problemPaths(err error) []string {
	var ve *schema.ValidationError
	if !errors.As(err, &ve) {
		return nil
	}
	out := make([]string, len(ve.Problems))
	for i, p := range ve.Problems {
		out[i] = p.Path
	}
	return out
}

func TestValidateOverridesMergesOverTheTemplate(t *testing.T) {

func TestValidateOverridesRejects(t *testing.T) {
	f, tmpl := overrideFixture(t)
	cases := []struct {

		paths []string
	}{

		{"wrong type", templates.Overrides{ExtraVars: map[string]any{"replicas": "three"}}, []string{"replicas"}},
		{"required var cleared", templates.Overrides{ExtraVars: map[string]any{"region": nil}}, []string{"region"}},
		{"connection security var", templates.Overrides{ExtraVars: map[string]any{"ansible_ssh_common_args": "-o X"}}, []string{"ansible_ssh_common_args"}},
	}

			if got := problemPaths(err); strings.Join(got, ",") != strings.Join(c.paths, ",") {
				t.Fatalf("err = %v, problem paths %v, want %v", err, got, c.paths)
			}

	if _, err := f.svc.ValidateOverrides(ctx, f.org, tmpl.ID, templates.Overrides{Limit: strptr(strings.Repeat("a", 501))}, templates.OverrideOptions{}); !errors.Is(err, templates.ErrInvalidInput) {

func TestValidateOverridesSealsAndRevealsSecrets(t *testing.T) {
	f, tmpl := overrideFixture(t)
	aad := []byte("schedule:test")
	stored, err := f.svc.ValidateOverrides(ctx, f.org, tmpl.ID, templates.Overrides{
		ExtraVars: map[string]any{"token": "override-secret"},
	}, templates.OverrideOptions{SealAAD: aad})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)
	}
	if _, sealed := schema.SealedBytes(stored.ExtraVars["token"]); !sealed {
		t.Fatalf("token = %#v, want a sealed envelope", stored.ExtraVars["token"])
	}
	// A later save that keeps the secret (the masked marker a read returned)
	// resolves against the stored override, not the template.
	kept, err := f.svc.ValidateOverrides(ctx, f.org, tmpl.ID, templates.Overrides{
		ExtraVars: map[string]any{"token": schema.Mask()},
	}, templates.OverrideOptions{SealAAD: aad, Stored: stored.ExtraVars})
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)

	revealed, err := f.svc.RevealOverrides(ctx, f.org, tmpl.ID, kept, aad)
	if err != nil {
		t.Fatal(err)

	if revealed.ExtraVars["token"] != "override-secret" {
		t.Fatalf("revealed = %#v", revealed.ExtraVars)
	}
	// The wrong AAD never opens it.
	if _, err := f.svc.RevealOverrides(ctx, f.org, tmpl.ID, kept, []byte("schedule:other")); err == nil {
		t.Fatal("RevealOverrides opened a value sealed for another schedule")
	}
}
```

```go
// validateLimit applies the limit rules a template and a run override share.
func validateLimit(limit string) error {
	if len(limit) > maxLimit || strings.ContainsRune(limit, 0) {
		return fmt.Errorf("%w: limit must be at most %d characters and contain no NUL", ErrInvalidInput, maxLimit)
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Implement `overrides.go`**

```go
// Per-run overrides of a template's limit and top-level extra vars: the
// one validation path schedules (stored, secrets sealed) and webhook
// triggers (mapped from a payload, secrets refused) share, so an override
// passes exactly the checks a template save applies (spec 2026-09-18 §2,
// §3): the limit rules, the connection-security denylist (2.36) and — with
// a schema — strict validation of the merged vars.
package templates

import (
	"context"
	"fmt"
	"maps"

	"github.com/google/uuid"

	"github.com/noixdev/pulsix/internal/ansiblevars"
	"github.com/noixdev/pulsix/internal/inventory/schema"
)

// Overrides replaces a template's limit and top-level extra vars for one
// run. A nil Limit keeps the template's; an extra var replaces the
// template's value of the same top-level name.
type Overrides struct {
	Limit     *string        `json:"limit,omitempty"`
	ExtraVars map[string]any `json:"extra_vars,omitempty"`
}

// Empty reports whether the overrides change nothing.
func (o Overrides) Empty() bool { return o.Limit == nil && len(o.ExtraVars) == 0 }

// OverrideOptions selects the treatment of secret-typed values.
type OverrideOptions struct {
	// SealAAD, when set, seals plaintext secret values under this AAD —
	// the storage form a schedule keeps.
	SealAAD []byte
	// Stored are the previously stored override vars; keep and mask
	// markers resolve against them (never against the template).
	Stored map[string]any
	// RefuseSecrets makes any secret-typed value a validation problem
	// (webhook payload mapping).
	RefuseSecrets bool
}

// ValidateOverrides checks in against the template and returns it in the
// form the caller stores or enqueues: secrets sealed (SealAAD), refused
// (RefuseSecrets) or passed through as plaintext (neither — the reveal
// path, whose result goes straight into a sealed job payload).
func (s *Service) ValidateOverrides(ctx context.Context, orgID, templateID uuid.UUID, in Overrides, opt OverrideOptions) (Overrides, error) {
	t, err := s.store.Get(ctx, orgID, templateID)
	if err != nil {
		return Overrides{}, err
	}
	out := Overrides{Limit: in.Limit}
	if in.Limit != nil {
		if err := validateLimit(*in.Limit); err != nil {
			return Overrides{}, err
		}
	}
	if len(in.ExtraVars) == 0 {
		return out, nil
	}
	if names := ansiblevars.ForbiddenIn(in.ExtraVars); len(names) > 0 {
		return Overrides{}, forbiddenProblems(names)
	}
	vars := maps.Clone(in.ExtraVars)
	if t.ExtraVarsSchema == nil {
		out.ExtraVars = vars
		return out, nil
	}
	fields := t.ExtraVarsSchema.Fields
	switch {
	case opt.RefuseSecrets:
		if problems := secretProblems(fields, vars); len(problems) > 0 {
			return Overrides{}, &schema.ValidationError{Problems: problems}
		}
	case opt.SealAAD != nil:
		if vars, err = s.sealSecrets(opt.SealAAD, fields, vars, opt.Stored); err != nil {
			return Overrides{}, err
		}
	}
	merged := maps.Clone(t.ExtraVars)
	if merged == nil {
		merged = map[string]any{}
	}
	maps.Copy(merged, vars)
	if err := schema.Validate(fields, merged); err != nil {
		return Overrides{}, err
	}
	out.ExtraVars = vars
	return out, nil
}

// RevealOverrides opens stored overrides (sealed under aad) and validates
// the plaintext against the template as it is now. The result carries
// plaintext secrets and must only ever travel into a sealed job payload.
func (s *Service) RevealOverrides(ctx context.Context, orgID, templateID uuid.UUID, stored Overrides, aad []byte) (Overrides, error) {
	t, err := s.store.Get(ctx, orgID, templateID)
	if err != nil {
		return Overrides{}, err
	}
	vars := stored.ExtraVars
	if t.ExtraVarsSchema != nil && len(vars) > 0 {
		vars, err = schema.MapSecrets(t.ExtraVarsSchema.Fields, vars, func(path string, v any) (any, error) {
			ct, ok := schema.SealedBytes(v)
			if !ok {
				return v, nil
			}
			plain, err := s.box.OpenAAD(ct, aad)
			if err != nil {
				return nil, fmt.Errorf("open override %s: %w", path, err)
			}
			return string(plain), nil
		})
		if err != nil {
			return Overrides{}, err
		}
	}
	return s.ValidateOverrides(ctx, orgID, templateID, Overrides{Limit: stored.Limit, ExtraVars: vars}, OverrideOptions{})
}

// SecretFields returns the top-level field names that are, or contain, a
// secret: webhook mappings may not target them (spec §3).
func SecretFields(sc *schema.Schema) map[string]bool {
	out := map[string]bool{}
	if sc == nil {
		return out
	}
	for _, f := range sc.Fields {
		if containsSecret(f) {
			out[f.Name] = true
		}
	}
	return out
}

func containsSecret(f schema.Field) bool {
	if f.Type == schema.TypeSecret {
		return true
	}
	if f.Item != nil && containsSecret(*f.Item) {
		return true
	}
	for _, sub := range f.Fields {
		if containsSecret(sub) {
			return true
		}
	}
	return false
}

func secretProblems(fields []schema.Field, vars map[string]any) []schema.Problem {
	var problems []schema.Problem
	_, _ = schema.MapSecrets(fields, vars, func(path string, v any) (any, error) {
		problems = append(problems, schema.Problem{Path: path, Message: "secret fields cannot be set from a webhook payload"})
		return v, nil
	})
	return problems
}
```

### Task 4: Triggered run requests in the job queue (`internal/jobs`)

This task adds the entry point schedules and webhooks enqueue through. It records the trigger kind and reference, seals the overrides into the job and merges them at claim time. It also adds the "is a run of this template / a sync of this repo still active" queries the overlap and `on_active` guards need.

**Files:**
- Create: `internal/db/migrations/00024_job_trigger_ref.sql`
- Create: `internal/jobs/run_request.go` (`RunOverrides`, `RunRequest`, `EnqueueRunWith`, `EnqueueSyncWith`, `HasActiveRun`, `HasActiveSync`), `internal/jobs/run_overrides.go` (claim-time `runOverrides` + `mergeExtraVars`)
- Modify: `internal/jobs/service.go`. Add the `TriggerSchedule`/`TriggerWebhook` constants and `Job.TriggerRef`. Give `enqueueRunScope` a `RunRequest` parameter instead of `actor`, set `Trigger`/`TriggerRef` from it and seal the payload. `EnqueueRun` and `RetryFailed` pass `RunRequest{Trigger: TriggerManual, Actor: actor}`. `EnqueueSync` becomes a one-line call of `EnqueueSyncWith`. The `Store` interface gains the two `HasActive…` methods.
- Modify: `internal/jobs/queue_sql.go`, in the `KindRun` branch of `buildPayload` only: call the new helpers (the diff is shown below).
- Modify: `internal/jobs/store_sql.go` (`createJobParams` sets `TriggerRef`; the row converters read `trigger_ref`; the two new store methods), `internal/db/queries/jobs.sql` (CreateJob column list, new queries, and `trigger_ref` in every explicit column list of GetJob/ListJobs), regenerated `internal/db/gen`
- Modify: `internal/api/job_handlers.go` (the `Trigger` enum becomes `manual,schedule,webhook,system` in `jobDTO` and `jobDetailDTO`; new `TriggerRef string \`json:"trigger_ref,omitempty"\`` in both), `web/src/api/schema.d.ts` (regenerated)
- Test: `internal/jobs/run_request_test.go` (external, fakes), `internal/jobs/run_overrides_test.go` (white-box, package `jobs`), `internal/jobs/run_request_store_test.go` (integration). `internal/jobs/service_test.go` (`fakeStore`) and `internal/jobs/rotation_test.go` (`rotationOnlyStore`, `plainRotationStore`) each gain the two `HasActive…` methods (return `false, nil` in the rotation fakes).

**Interfaces:**
- Produces (package `jobs`):

```go
const (
	TriggerSchedule = "schedule"
	TriggerWebhook  = "webhook"
)

// RunOverrides is the plaintext override a run carries sealed in its payload.
type RunOverrides struct {
	Limit     *string        `json:"limit,omitempty"`
	ExtraVars map[string]any `json:"extra_vars,omitempty"`
}

// RunRequest says who or what starts a run and with which overrides.
type RunRequest struct {
	Trigger    string     // TriggerManual | TriggerSchedule | TriggerWebhook | TriggerSystem
	TriggerRef *uuid.UUID // the schedule or webhook trigger id
	Actor      *uuid.UUID // the user, for manual runs and "run now"
	Overrides  *RunOverrides
}

func (s *Service) EnqueueRunWith(ctx context.Context, orgID, templateID uuid.UUID, req RunRequest) ([]Job, error)
func (s *Service) EnqueueSyncWith(ctx context.Context, orgID, repoID uuid.UUID, req RunRequest) (Job, error) // Overrides must be nil
func (s *Service) HasActiveRun(ctx context.Context, orgID, templateID uuid.UUID) (bool, error)
func (s *Service) HasActiveSync(ctx context.Context, orgID, repoID uuid.UUID) (bool, error)
```

- `Job` gains `TriggerRef *uuid.UUID`, and `Store` gains `HasActiveTemplateJob(ctx, orgID, templateID uuid.UUID) (bool, error)` and `HasActiveRepoSync(ctx, orgID, repoID uuid.UUID) (bool, error)`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the migration**

`internal/db/migrations/00024_job_trigger_ref.sql`:

```sql
-- +goose Up
-- Triggered runs (spec 2026-09-18 §2 "Actor and permissions"): jobs keep
-- their existing trigger column as the trigger kind — now constrained to
-- the four kinds — and gain the id of the schedule or webhook trigger that
-- started them. No foreign key: schedules and triggers are deleted freely
-- and runs outlive them, like template_id.
ALTER TABLE jobs ADD COLUMN trigger_ref uuid;
ALTER TABLE jobs ADD CONSTRAINT jobs_trigger_check
  CHECK (trigger IN ('manual', 'schedule', 'webhook', 'system'));

-- The overlap guard and the webhook on_active check ask "is a run of this
-- template still active?" on every due schedule and delivery.
CREATE INDEX jobs_active_template_idx ON jobs (org_id, template_id)
  WHERE template_id IS NOT NULL AND status NOT IN ('succeeded', 'failed', 'cancelled', 'error');

-- +goose Down
DROP INDEX jobs_active_template_idx;
ALTER TABLE jobs DROP CONSTRAINT jobs_trigger_check;
ALTER TABLE jobs DROP COLUMN trigger_ref;
```

Queries (append to `internal/db/queries/jobs.sql`):

```sql
-- name: HasActiveTemplateJob :one
SELECT EXISTS (
  SELECT 1 FROM jobs
  WHERE org_id = $1 AND template_id = $2
    AND status NOT IN ('succeeded', 'failed', 'cancelled', 'error')
);

-- name: HasActiveRepoSync :one
-- sync_repo payloads are plain JSON ({"repo_id": ...}), never sealed.
SELECT EXISTS (
  SELECT 1 FROM jobs
  WHERE org_id = sqlc.arg(org_id) AND kind = 'sync_repo'
    AND payload->>'repo_id' = sqlc.arg(repo_id)::text
    AND status NOT IN ('succeeded', 'failed', 'cancelled', 'error')
);
```

In `CreateJob`, add `trigger_ref` as the 14th column and `$14` as the 14th value. Add `trigger_ref` (or `j.trigger_ref`, matching the query's alias style) to every explicit select list in `jobs.sql` that feeds `toJob`/`toJobDetail`. `RETURNING *` and `SELECT *` pick it up by themselves. Then run `make sqlc`.

```go
func sealedOverride(t *testing.T, q *SQLQueue, job Job, ov RunOverrides) json.RawMessage {

func TestBuildPayloadMergesRunOverrides(t *testing.T) {
	org, zone, templateID := uuid.Must(uuid.NewV7()), uuid.Must(uuid.NewV7()), uuid.Must(uuid.NewV7())
	schemaRaw := json.RawMessage(`{"fields":[{"name":"token","type":"secret"},{"name":"region","type":"string"},{"name":"replicas","type":"int"}]}`)
	templates := fakeTemplates{byID: map[uuid.UUID]TemplateRow{templateID: {

	q.SetKeyPins(fakePins{})
	job := Job{ID: uuid.Must(uuid.NewV7()), OrgID: org, ZoneID: zone, Kind: KindRun, TemplateID: &templateID, HostIDs: testHosts}
	limit := "web*"
	job.Payload = sealedOverride(t, q, job, RunOverrides{Limit: &limit, ExtraVars: map[string]any{"replicas": 3, "token": "override-token-9f2"}})

	out := &workerpb.Job{}
	if err := q.buildPayload(context.Background(), job, out); err != nil {
		t.Fatalf("buildPayload: %v", err)
	}
	if out.Limit != "web*" {
		t.Fatalf("limit = %q", out.Limit)
	}
	if out.ExtraVars["region"] != `"eu"` || out.ExtraVars["replicas"] != "3" || out.ExtraVars["token"] != `"override-token-9f2"` {
		t.Fatalf("extra vars = %v", out.ExtraVars)
	}
	if !strings.Contains(strings.Join(out.Redact, "\n"), "override-token-9f2") {
		t.Fatalf("a secret-typed override must be redacted: %v", out.Redact)
	}
	if out.KindPayload != "" {
		t.Fatalf("run overrides must not leak into kind_payload: %q", out.KindPayload)
	}
}

func TestBuildPayloadRefusesDenylistedOverride(t *testing.T) {
	org, templateID := uuid.Must(uuid.NewV7()), uuid.Must(uuid.NewV7())
	templates := fakeTemplates{byID: map[uuid.UUID]TemplateRow{templateID: {ID: templateID, OrgID: org, PlaybookInline: "- hosts: all\n"}}}

	if err := q.buildPayload(context.Background(), job, &workerpb.Job{}); err == nil {
		t.Fatal("a denylisted override var must fail the claim")
	}
}

func TestMergeExtraVars(t *testing.T) {
	got, err := mergeExtraVars(json.RawMessage(`{"a":1,"b":2}`), &RunOverrides{ExtraVars: map[string]any{"b": 3, "c": "x"}})
	if err != nil || string(got) != `{"a":1,"b":3,"c":"x"}` {
		t.Fatalf("merge = %s, %v", got, err)
	}
	if got, _ := mergeExtraVars(json.RawMessage(`{"a":1}`), nil); string(got) != `{"a":1}` {
		t.Fatalf("nil overrides = %s", got)
	}
	if got, _ := mergeExtraVars(nil, &RunOverrides{ExtraVars: map[string]any{"a": 1}}); string(got) != `{"a":1}` {
		t.Fatalf("empty base = %s", got)
	}
	if _, err := mergeExtraVars(json.RawMessage(`[]`), &RunOverrides{ExtraVars: map[string]any{"a": 1}}); err == nil {
		t.Fatal("a non-object base must fail")
	}
}
```
