### Task 17: Milestone E2E (token enrollment, a schedule on an injected clock, a signed webhook, revocation)

This is the v0.2c gate. It uses the same fully wired manager as the v0.2b gate (`internal/app.Build`, real HTTP API, worker gRPC over loopback mTLS, TimescaleDB testcontainer, SSH host behind a bastion). What changes is the worker, which **enrolls with a one-time token created over the API**. The scenario then asserts, in order:
- the token works once
- the worker is listed online with a certificate of about 90 days
- a cron schedule fires when the injected ticker clock reaches `next_run_at`, and its job carries `trigger=schedule` and `trigger_ref=<schedule>` and succeeds on the token-enrolled worker
- a correctly signed webhook answers 202 with a run that succeeds, and a wrong signature answers the uniform 404
- revocation flips the worker to `revoked`, a direct `ClaimJob` with its identity is `Unauthenticated`, a new run stays `queued` (the zone has no other worker) and the revoked worker can be deleted

CI needs no change. The `e2e-ansible` job already runs `go test -race -count=1 ./internal/e2e/...` in fake and in real ansible mode, and the scenario's playbook is the v0.2b fixture.

**Files:**
- Create: `internal/e2e/automation_test.go`
- Modify: `internal/e2e/ansible_test.go` (`wiring` options, `enrollWithCLI` split out, the gRPC listener bound before `Build` so `GRPCPublicAddr` names it, `scenario.workerCfg`)

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `app.Build(ctx, cfg, opts ...app.Option)` and `app.WithScheduleClock(func() time.Time)` (Task 6), `config.Config.ScheduleTick` / `GRPCPublicAddr` (Tasks 1, 6, 15), `worker.EnsureIdentity(ctx, cfg, log)` and `worker.Config.{IdentityDir, EnrollToken}` (Task 14), `POST /worker-enrollments`, `GET /workers`, `POST /workers/{id}/revoke`, `DELETE /workers/{id}` (Task 15), `POST /schedules` and `GET /schedules/{id}/runs` (Task 7), `GET /jobs?group=` with `trigger`/`trigger_ref` (Tasks 4 and 8), `POST /triggers` → `{trigger, secret, url}` (Task 11), `POST /api/v1/hooks/jobs/{id}` → 202 `{run_id}`, and 404 for a bad signature (Task 10).
- Produces: `wireScenario(t, ctx, cancel, wiring{tokenWorker, scheduleTick, opts})`. `wiring{}` is the unchanged v0.2b gate.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing scenario**

`internal/e2e/automation_test.go`:

```go
// The v0.2c milestone gate: the same fully wired manager as the v0.2b
// gate, but the worker enrolls with a one-time token over the API, a
// schedule fires on an injected clock, a signed webhook starts a run, and a
// revocation cuts the worker off.
package e2e

import (
	"bytes"
	"context"
	"crypto/hmac"
	"crypto/sha256"
	"encoding/hex"
	"encoding/json"
	"net/http"
	"os/exec"
	"path/filepath"
	"strings"
	"sync"
	"testing"
	"time"

	"google.golang.org/grpc/codes"
	"google.golang.org/grpc/status"

	"github.com/noixdev/pulsix/internal/app"
	"github.com/noixdev/pulsix/internal/pb/workerpb"
	"github.com/noixdev/pulsix/internal/worker"
)

// tokenWorkerName is the name the token-enrolled worker is registered as.
const tokenWorkerName = "e2e-token-worker"

// fakeClock is the schedule ticker's clock: real time until the test moves
// it, so everything else in the manager keeps its wall-clock behaviour.
type fakeClock struct {
	mu     sync.Mutex
	offset time.Duration
}

func (c *fakeClock) Now() time.Time {
	c.mu.Lock()
	defer c.mu.Unlock()
	return time.Now().Add(c.offset)
}

// setAt moves the clock so that Now() reads at (and keeps running from there).
func (c *fakeClock) setAt(at time.Time) {
	c.mu.Lock()
	defer c.mu.Unlock()
	c.offset = time.Until(at)
}

type (
	workersOut struct {
		Workers []struct {
			ID            string     `json:"id"`
			Name          string     `json:"name"`
			Status        string     `json:"status"`
			CertExpiresAt *time.Time `json:"cert_expires_at,omitempty"`
		} `json:"workers"`
	}
	scheduleRunOut struct {
		Outcome    string `json:"outcome"`
		Reason     string `json:"reason,omitempty"`
		JobGroupID string `json:"job_group_id,omitempty"`
	}
	scheduleOut struct {
		Schedule struct {
			ID        string     `json:"id"`
			NextRunAt *time.Time `json:"next_run_at,omitempty"`
		} `json:"schedule"`
	}
	jobListOut struct {
		Jobs []struct {
			ID         string `json:"id"`
			Status     string `json:"status"`
			Trigger    string `json:"trigger"`
			TriggerRef string `json:"trigger_ref,omitempty"`
		} `json:"jobs"`
	}
	triggerCreatedOut struct {
		Trigger struct {
			ID string `json:"id"`
		} `json:"trigger"`
		Secret string `json:"secret"`
		URL    string `json:"url"`
	}
	hookOut struct {
		RunID string `json:"run_id"`
	}
)

func TestAutomationEndToEnd(t *testing.T) {
	if _, err := exec.LookPath("docker"); err != nil {
		t.Skipf("no docker for the testcontainer database: %v", err)
	}
	ctx, cancel := context.WithCancel(context.Background())
	t.Cleanup(cancel)
	clock := &fakeClock{}
	s := wireScenario(t, ctx, cancel, wiring{
		tokenWorker:  true,
		scheduleTick: 100 * time.Millisecond,
		opts:         []app.Option{app.WithScheduleClock(clock.Now)},
	})
	s.automate(ctx, clock)
}

// enrollWithToken is the v0.2c path: an owner creates a one-time token over
// the API and the worker enrolls with it — a fresh key, a CSR, the CA
// pinned by the token's fingerprint. The token works exactly once.
func (s *scenario) enrollWithToken(ctx context.Context, managerAddr string) worker.Config {
	s.t.Helper()
	var created struct {
		Token string `json:"token"`
	}
	s.post("/api/v1/"+s.apiPath()+"/worker-enrollments", map[string]any{"zone_id": s.zoneID, "name": tokenWorkerName}, &created)
	if !strings.HasPrefix(created.Token, "pxw1.") {
		s.t.Fatalf("enrollment token = %q, want the pxw1 format", created.Token)
	}
	identityAt := func(dir string) worker.Config {
		return worker.Config{
			ManagerAddr: managerAddr,
			IdentityDir: dir,
			CertPath:    filepath.Join(dir, "cert.pem"),
			KeyPath:     filepath.Join(dir, "key.pem"),
			CAPath:      filepath.Join(dir, "ca.pem"),
			EnrollToken: created.Token,
			Version:     "e2e",
		}
	}
	cfg := identityAt(filepath.Join(s.t.TempDir(), "identity"))
	if err := worker.EnsureIdentity(ctx, cfg, testLogger(s.t)); err != nil {
		s.t.Fatalf("enroll with token: %v", err)
	}
	// Single use: a second enrollment with the same token is refused.
	if err := worker.EnsureIdentity(ctx, identityAt(filepath.Join(s.t.TempDir(), "again")), testLogger(s.t)); err == nil {
		s.t.Fatal("a second enrollment with the same token succeeded")
	}
	return cfg
}

// automate drives the v0.2c steps after the wiring: the worker list, a
// schedule firing on the injected clock, a signed webhook, and revocation.
func (s *scenario) automate(ctx context.Context, clock *fakeClock) {
	s.t.Helper()
	prefix := "/api/v1/" + s.apiPath()

	// --- The token-enrolled worker is listed and has claimed ---------------
	// The wiring's repository sync already ran on it, so it is online.
	var list workersOut
	s.get(prefix+"/workers", &list)
	if len(list.Workers) != 1 || list.Workers[0].Name != tokenWorkerName || list.Workers[0].Status != "online" {
		s.t.Fatalf("workers = %+v, want the one online token worker", list.Workers)
	}
	workerID := list.Workers[0].ID
	if exp := list.Workers[0].CertExpiresAt; exp == nil || time.Until(*exp) < 89*24*time.Hour || time.Until(*exp) > 91*24*time.Hour {
		s.t.Fatalf("certificate expiry = %v, want about 90 days", exp)
	}

	// --- Host key pin, playbook and template --------------------------------
	s.pinHostKey()
	s.helloPB = s.createEditorPlaybook("hello", greetTemplate(s.t))
	s.helloTID = s.createTemplate("hello", s.helloPB, map[string]any{"greeting": s.greeting})

	// --- A schedule fires on the injected clock -----------------------------
	var sched scheduleOut
	s.post(prefix+"/schedules", map[string]any{
		"template_id": s.helloTID, "name": "every five minutes", "mode": "cron",
		"cron": "*/5 * * * *", "timezone": "Europe/Berlin",
	}, &sched)
	if sched.Schedule.NextRunAt == nil {
		s.t.Fatal("the new schedule carries no next_run_at")
	}
	clock.setAt(*sched.Schedule.NextRunAt)
	fired := s.awaitScheduleRun(sched.Schedule.ID, 30*time.Second)
	if fired.Outcome != "triggered" || fired.JobGroupID == "" {
		s.t.Fatalf("schedule run = %+v, want triggered with a job group", fired)
	}
	var group jobListOut
	s.get(prefix+"/jobs?group="+fired.JobGroupID, &group)
	if len(group.Jobs) != 1 || group.Jobs[0].Trigger != "schedule" || group.Jobs[0].TriggerRef != sched.Schedule.ID {
		s.t.Fatalf("scheduled jobs = %+v, want one job triggered by schedule %s", group.Jobs, sched.Schedule.ID)
	}
	scheduled := s.awaitJob(group.Jobs[0].ID, "run", 90*time.Second)
	if scheduled.Job.Status != "succeeded" || scheduled.Job.WorkerName != tokenWorkerName {
		s.t.Fatalf("scheduled run = %q on %q (error %q), want succeeded on %s%s",
			scheduled.Job.Status, scheduled.Job.WorkerName, scheduled.Job.Error, tokenWorkerName, s.runnerOutput())
	}

	// --- A signed webhook starts a run ---------------------------------------
	var trig triggerCreatedOut
	s.post(prefix+"/triggers", map[string]any{
		"kind": "template_run", "template_id": s.helloTID, "name": "ci", "preset": "pulsix",
	}, &trig)
	runID := s.postHook(trig.URL, trig.Secret, []byte(`{"ref":"refs/heads/main"}`))
	hooked := s.awaitJob(runID, "run", 90*time.Second)
	if hooked.Job.Status != "succeeded" {
		s.t.Fatalf("webhook run = %q (error %q)%s", hooked.Job.Status, hooked.Job.Error, s.runnerOutput())
	}
	// A wrong signature answers the same uniform 404 as an unknown id.
	if code := s.postHookStatus(trig.URL, "sha256="+strings.Repeat("0", 64), []byte(`{}`)); code != http.StatusNotFound {
		s.t.Fatalf("hook with a wrong signature = %d, want 404", code)
	}

	// --- Revocation ------------------------------------------------------------
	var revoked struct {
		Worker struct {
			Status string `json:"status"`
		} `json:"worker"`
	}
	s.post(prefix+"/workers/"+workerID+"/revoke", struct{}{}, &revoked)
	if revoked.Worker.Status != "revoked" {
		s.t.Fatalf("revoked worker status = %q", revoked.Worker.Status)
	}
	// Its identity no longer authenticates: a direct claim is refused.
	conn, err := worker.Dial(ctx, s.workerCfg)
	if err != nil {
		s.t.Fatal(err)
	}
	defer func() { _ = conn.Close() }()
	_, err = workerpb.NewWorkerServiceClient(conn).ClaimJob(ctx, &workerpb.ClaimJobRequest{Version: "e2e", ZoneIds: []string{s.zoneID}})
	if status.Code(err) != codes.Unauthenticated {
		s.t.Fatalf("claim with a revoked certificate = %v, want Unauthenticated", err)
	}
	// With the zone's only worker revoked, a new run stays queued.
	queuedID := s.runTemplate(s.helloTID)
	time.Sleep(2 * time.Second) // twenty claim intervals of the (refused) loop
	var queued jobDetailOut
	s.get(prefix+"/jobs/"+queuedID, &queued)
	if queued.Job.Status != "queued" {
		s.t.Fatalf("run after revocation = %q, want queued", queued.Job.Status)
	}
	// A revoked worker can be deleted.
	s.api.do(http.MethodDelete, prefix+"/workers/"+workerID, nil, nil)
	s.get(prefix+"/workers", &list)
	if len(list.Workers) != 0 {
		s.t.Fatalf("workers after delete = %+v, want none", list.Workers)
	}
}

// pinHostKey runs one connection test and pins the host key it observed —
// runs refuse a host whose key is unknown.
func (s *scenario) pinHostKey() {
	s.t.Helper()
	prefix := "/api/v1/" + s.apiPath()
	var started struct {
		JobID string `json:"job_id"`
	}
	s.post(prefix+"/hosts/"+s.hostID+"/connection-test", struct{}{}, &started)
	if job := s.awaitJob(started.JobID, "connection_test", 60*time.Second); job.Job.Status != "succeeded" {
		s.t.Fatalf("connection test = %q (error %q)", job.Job.Status, job.Job.Error)
	}
	var test hostTestOut
	s.get(prefix+"/hosts/"+s.hostID+"/connection-test", &test)
	s.put(prefix+"/hosts/"+s.hostID+"/host-key", map[string]any{"fingerprint": test.Test.HostKey}, &map[string]any{})
}

// awaitScheduleRun polls the schedule's run history until the ticker
// recorded its first decision.
func (s *scenario) awaitScheduleRun(id string, timeout time.Duration) scheduleRunOut {
	s.t.Helper()
	deadline := time.Now().Add(timeout)
	for {
		var out struct {
			Runs []scheduleRunOut `json:"runs"`
		}
		s.get("/api/v1/"+s.apiPath()+"/schedules/"+id+"/runs", &out)
		if len(out.Runs) > 0 {
			return out.Runs[0]
		}
		if time.Now().After(deadline) {
			s.t.Fatalf("schedule %s recorded no run in %s", id, timeout)
		}
		time.Sleep(100 * time.Millisecond)
	}
}

// postHook sends a correctly signed delivery and returns the started run.
func (s *scenario) postHook(url, secret string, body []byte) string {
	s.t.Helper()
	mac := hmac.New(sha256.New, []byte(secret))
	mac.Write(body)
	req := s.hookRequest(url, "sha256="+hex.EncodeToString(mac.Sum(nil)), body)
	resp, err := http.DefaultClient.Do(req)
	if err != nil {
		s.t.Fatal(err)
	}
	defer func() { _ = resp.Body.Close() }()
	if resp.StatusCode != http.StatusAccepted {
		s.t.Fatalf("hook delivery = %d, want 202", resp.StatusCode)
	}
	var out hookOut
	if err := json.NewDecoder(resp.Body).Decode(&out); err != nil || out.RunID == "" {
		s.t.Fatalf("hook delivery answered no run_id (%v)", err)
	}
	return out.RunID
}

// postHookStatus sends a delivery with the given signature header value.
func (s *scenario) postHookStatus(url, signature string, body []byte) int {
	s.t.Helper()
	resp, err := http.DefaultClient.Do(s.hookRequest(url, signature, body))
	if err != nil {
		s.t.Fatal(err)
	}
	_ = resp.Body.Close()
	return resp.StatusCode
}

// hookRequest is a webhook delivery: no session cookie, only the signature.
func (s *scenario) hookRequest(url, signature string, body []byte) *http.Request {
	req, err := http.NewRequest(http.MethodPost, url, bytes.NewReader(body))
	if err != nil {
		s.t.Fatal(err)
	}
	req.Header.Set("Content-Type", "application/json")
	req.Header.Set("X-Pulsix-Signature-256", signature)
	return req
}
```

+// enrollWithCLI is the v0.2b path: `pulsix-manager worker add` against the
+// same database and master key issues the client certificate from the
+// instance CA (the same one Build's gRPC server verifies against) and
+// registers the serial — the exact output the worker binary is configured
+// from.
+func enrollWithCLI(t *testing.T, ctx context.Context, masterKey [32]byte, org string) worker.Config {
+	t.Helper()
+	enrollCfg := config.Config{DatabaseURL: testdb.URL(t), MasterKey: masterKey, GRPCListen: "localhost:0"}
+	var enroll strings.Builder
+	if err := app.RunWorkerAdd(ctx, enrollCfg, app.WorkerAddOptions{ZoneSlug: "default", OrgSlug: org, Name: "e2e-worker"}, &enroll, io.Discard); err != nil {
+		t.Fatalf("worker add: %v", err)
+	}
+	certPEM, keyPEM, caPEM, err := ParseEnrollmentBlocks(enroll.String())
+	if err != nil {
+		t.Fatal(err)
+	}
+	dir := t.TempDir()
+	cfg := worker.Config{
+		CertPath: filepath.Join(dir, "worker.crt"),
+		KeyPath:  filepath.Join(dir, "worker.key"),
+		CAPath:   filepath.Join(dir, "ca.pem"),
+	}
+	for path, content := range map[string]string{cfg.CertPath: certPEM, cfg.KeyPath: keyPEM, cfg.CAPath: caPEM} {
+		if err := os.WriteFile(path, []byte(content), 0o600); err != nil {
+			t.Fatal(err)
+		}
+	}
+	return cfg
+}
+
 // testLogger keeps the worker's structured logs quiet unless the test
 // verbosely runs.
 func testLogger(t *testing.T) *slog.Logger {

- [ ] **Step 4: The architecture spec, §7 triggers**

Replace the two bullets from `- Triggers per template: **manual**` through the end of the `- Schedule notifications:` bullet (the one ending in `rate_limit)`).`) with:

```markdown
- Triggers: **manual** (button, API), **schedule** and **incoming webhook**. Every run
  records `jobs.trigger` (`manual`, `schedule`, `webhook`, `system`) and `trigger_ref`
  (the schedule or webhook trigger), and the audit log names its actor: a user, a
  token, `schedule:<id>`, or `webhook:<id>` with the source address.
- Schedules (v0.2c): cron (5 fields and `@hourly`, `@daily`, `@weekly`, `@monthly`;
  day-of-month and day-of-week combine with OR) or a simple mode (every N minutes or
  hours, daily, weekly, monthly) compiled to cron; an IANA time zone with defined DST
  behaviour (a time in the spring-forward gap runs at the first valid minute after it,
  a repeated fall-back time runs once); guards `overlap` (skip or queue) and
  `catch_up` (enabled, window 1 min–24 h); per-schedule `limit` and extra-var
  overrides validated against the template schema, secret values sealed; pause,
  resume, run now, pause all. A ticker (`PULSIX_SCHEDULE_TICK`, default 15 s) takes
  due schedules with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, so with several managers each
  occurrence is decided once; enqueueing is at-least-once. Every decision is kept in
  `schedule_runs` (`triggered`, `skipped_overlap`, `missed`, `failed`). Scheduled runs
  pass the same enqueue validation as manual runs. The maintenance-window guard
  arrives with maintenance windows (v0.2d).
- Incoming webhooks (v0.2c): `POST /api/v1/hooks/jobs/{id}` runs a template and
  `POST /api/v1/hooks/repos/{id}` syncs a repository (the push webhook of 2.8).
  Presets: Pulsix, GitHub and Gitea (HMAC-SHA256 of the raw body), GitLab and bearer
  (token). An optional timestamp header limits replays to ±5 minutes, and the HMAC
  then covers `<timestamp>.<body>`. An IP allowlist applies to the client address that
  `PULSIX_TRUSTED_PROXIES` resolves. The mapping takes an RFC 9535 JSONPath subset (`$`,
  names, indexes, `[*]`) from the JSON payload to extra vars and `limit`, validated
  like a manual run; secret-typed fields and the connection-security variables cannot
  be mapped. A per-trigger token bucket is shared by all managers, with a per-address
  limit in front of it. `on_active` either queues or skips. A trigger keeps its last
  500 deliveries (`trigger_deliveries`) without the body.
- Schedule notifications (v0.2d): rules per schedule (`on failure`, `on change`, `on
  success`, `skipped/missed` → channels from §11), an optional weekly digest, and a
  calendar of scheduled runs together with maintenance windows.
```

- [ ] **Step 5: The architecture spec, §14 security**

After the `- **Worker trust model.**` bullet, insert:

```markdown
- **Worker enrollment (v0.2c).** An owner or admin creates a token
  `pxw1.<id>.<secret>.<ca-sha256>`: single use, valid for one hour, bound to
  organization, zone and worker name; only the SHA-256 of the secret is stored, and
  it is compared in constant time. The worker creates its key locally (it never
  leaves the host), sends a CSR over a connection pinned to the CA fingerprint in the
  token, and receives a 90-day certificate whose CN and organization/zone URI SANs
  the CA sets itself (CSR subject and extensions are ignored). It renews over its
  authenticated connection once a third of the validity is left, keeping its key.
  `Enroll` is the only RPC callable without a client certificate and is limited to
  10 calls per minute and address. Revocation marks the worker and every serial it
  ever held as revoked, ends its open event streams and requeues its jobs; unknown
  and revoked serials are `Unauthenticated`, and a certificate whose zone differs from
  the registry row is refused. A revoked worker cannot come back under a recreated
  row, because its serials stay revoked.
- **Incoming webhooks (v0.2c).** The hook endpoints are the only unauthenticated
  write paths besides login, setup and enrollment: body ≤ 1 MiB, constant-time
  signature and token compares, one identical empty 404 for unknown, disabled,
  disallowed-address and bad-signature calls, per-trigger and per-address rate
  limits, secrets sealed with AAD `trigger:<org>:<id>` and shown only at creation and
  rotation, and no secrets or bodies in URLs, logs, audit entries or delivery rows.
  Webhook and scheduled runs pass the same validation as manual runs (schema, the
  connection-security denylist, host-key pins). Outbound webhooks (notification
  channels, with SSRF rules) are v0.2d.
```

## v0.2c follow-ups

Decisions and limits recorded while planning v0.2c (2026-09-18).

| # | Item | Status | Notes |
|---|------|--------|-------|
| 2.54 | A revocation cuts open event streams on every manager | planned | v0.6: today only the manager that served the revocation cuts the worker's streams; every manager refuses its next RPC |
| 2.55 | Retry-failed of a triggered run keeps the run's overrides | idea | overrides travel sealed in the job payload, which is wiped when the job ends; a retry uses the template's own limit and extra vars (documented) |
| 2.56 | Delete job templates from the SPA | planned | the API can delete a template, which cascades to its schedules and webhook triggers; the SPA has no delete action yet |
| 2.57 | Stop a revoked worker's running ansible process | idea | the manager cannot reach into the worker; the revoked worker's results are refused and the job is requeued elsewhere |
| 2.58 | Per-address limits shared across managers | idea | the hook and enrollment per-address limits count per manager; the per-trigger token bucket is already shared |

**Found during the dry run and folded into the tasks:**
